# History and decisions

This file keeps the long-form material that used to live in `AGENTS.md`: the
detailed project narrative, the decision index and the dated status log. It is a
historical record. `AGENTS.md` carries only current operational facts, `adr/`
carries per-decision detail and `specs/` carries normative behavior.

## Detailed project narrative

`igis-v2` is a from-scratch second version of a personal operating system whose first
version was written in 2005 as a front-end for a modem. It began as
a Linux driver, the user/kernel ring boundary got in the way, and it grew into a kernel
with its own boot path. No code survives; the continuity is design intent and the
constraints learned there.

- **Primary language:** Rust (`no_std`, freestanding).
- **First architecture:** x86_64. All ISA-specific code is isolated so additional
  architectures can be added without touching the kernel core (ADR 0013).
- **Central thesis:** the kernel is a programmable system — a language runtime plus
  standard library owning the machine. Applications are source code, not native
  binaries, compiled in the kernel at load and never precompiled (ADR 0019). Everything
  runs at ring 0 in one address space.
- **Execution model:** one interpreter loop per core; tasks are claimed from a global
  runnable set and are never pinned to a core (ADR 0011).
- **Long-term goal:** a general personal system. The first milestone proves the thesis
  with a booting kernel, source applications, a read-only application image and a
  persistent writable `/home`, and a REPL that can drive a device.
- **Status:** implementation. The current state (specs 0001–0022, M2–M12, 613 host
  tests) is in [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md); the snapshot below is the M10-era narrative.
  At that point specs 0001–0020 were accepted (0003 core implemented; 0015–0019
  implemented; 0020's M10.1–M10.9 and the blocking follow-up implemented; **M10
  complete**), **608 host tests** (530 language + 56 `net` + 22 `ext2w`), ABI v1 at
  **75** opcodes, M5/M6/M7.1/M8.1–M8.9 complete and
  M9.1–M9.4 (editor syntax highlighting, cheap pointer redraws, a draggable
  scrollbar, a `/lib` file-open dialog and a 128 KiB document buffer) done. The
  application
  image is a read-only ext2 tree (`lib`, `base`, `home`, `system`) mounted as a GRUB
  module; `/home` is a separate 8 MiB ext2 image on an ATA disk, mounted read-write
  through `ext2w` and one path router (persistent; `e2fsck -fn` clean). The read
  builtins are 41–43 and the write builtins are 53–57
  (`fs_write`/`fs_remove`/`fs_mkdir`/`fs_rename`/`fs_truncate`, `home/` only). Boot is
  `system/entrypoint` → `base/shell`; the language compiles source with
  `compile_with_imports` (`/lib` modules, package-wide nominal types). The kernel boots under
  GRUB/multiboot2 into long mode, prints through a framebuffer console mirrored to serial,
  has a GDT/TSS and an exception path with a double-fault IST, runs a 1 kHz APIC timer
  driving a global nanosecond clock, and owns a bitmap frame allocator. The full vertical
  slice now runs: `xtask image` builds a 4 MiB ext2 image from `samples/apps/` and attaches
  it as a GRUB module, the kernel mounts it read-only, boots `system/entrypoint` (falling
  back to `base/shell`), compiles the source in the kernel with
  `compiler::compile_with_imports` (a `/lib` module is read through the same ext2 mount),
  places
  `code`/`state`/`stack` in contiguous frames, verifies the package and enqueues the task
  in a 64-slot global runnable set. The boot core then runs the scheduler loop: claim by
  value move under a per-slot spin lock, a 10 000-bytecode quantum, per-task CPU accounting
  with a 10 s / 200 000 000-step watchdog, and release or deterministic termination. The
  boot application crosses many quanta, prints its output, exits `Ok`, and the system
  prints the claim/yield/exit summary and halts. The compiler is `no_std` and
  allocation-free; the scheduler path adds no heap. The REPL request service now runs: a
  1024-slot static queue is serviced before every claim, `core.spawn` reserves a global
  task id, `core.wait` blocks the parent (a `Blocked` slot skipped by `claim`) until the
  child's exit record wakes it, and a 64-entry exit ring serves `wait`/`run`. These
  primitives use the predeclared `Status`/`Diagnostic`/`ExitReason` shapes (spec 0005,
  "Implemented ABI"). Console input is a raw byte stream now (Q44, ADR 0037): COM1 drains
  into a 512-byte ring, the kernel neither echoes nor assembles lines, `core.read_input`
  returns whatever bytes are waiting without blocking, and the shell echoes and edits its
  own `string[256]` with the `stdlib.str_*` helpers; PS/2 key presses are events only.
  Buffers can be indexed and compared: `buf[i]` reads a raw `byte` (checked
  against the runtime length), `bytes[i] := b` writes one byte (checked against the
  capacity, extending the length and zero-filling the gap), `=`/`<>` compare two buffers
  of the same kind across capacities, and `core.len` returns the runtime length. The shell
  is now a real command loop (`help`, `echo <text>`, `run <app> [args]`, `exit`): `core.find`
  locates the first space, `core.substr_into` splits the line and the `run` tail, and the
  child prints the argument it received, so `run child world` proves argument carriage. The
  host console scripts those four commands as a byte queue so `langhost` runs the same
  application-side line editing deterministically. Integer formatting now exists as the staged `core.format_int_into`
  (decimal/hex, sign, zero padding, exact-fit `-6`), and the first `syslib` primitives —
  `port_in8`/`port_out8` with `Port` staged as a scalar `uint` — let an application drive
  hardware: `samples/apps/clock` polls the CMOS RTC update flag, reads seconds/minutes/hours
  and prints `HH:MM:SS` (`run clock` in the shell). Compilation is now bounded and runs on a
  guarded stack: the parser and the recursive codegen walks reject deeper than 32 counted
  levels with a `Diagnostic`, and after early boot `kmain` switches from the `.boot` stack
  to a 256 KiB stack with a not-present guard frame below it (the guard's 2 MiB identity
  page is split to 4 KiB PTEs; `#PF` uses its own IST). A stack overflow now faults cleanly
  at the guard instead of writing into the boot page tables (Q42). Enums are implemented
  (`type Name = (Variant, ...)`, nominal, one 8-byte ordinal, checked `EnumName(int)`);
  `Status` and `FaultCode` are predeclared; records are implemented
  (`type Name = record field: T; ... end;`: nominal, declaration-order 8-byte-aligned
  layout, nested records, buffer fields, whole-record copies, `value`/`const`/`var` record
  parameters). Sum types and pattern matching are implemented
  (`type Name = Variant | Variant(payload: T);`: nominal, 8-byte tag at 0 plus the largest
  payload at 8, destination-driven construction, exhaustive `case` with an arm-local
  by-value payload binding). `Diagnostic` is a predeclared record and `ExitReason` is the
  predeclared sum type `Ok | Killed | Faulted(code: FaultCode)` at the fixed `abi::layout`
  offsets: `core.spawn(path, args, var diag, var id)` fills `diag` on a synchronous
  failure, `core.wait(id, var reason)` writes the reason's tag/payload, and the shell
  prints `diag.message` and reports the reason with a `case`. `core.run(path, args,
  var diag, var reason)` now composes spawn + wait with the pending id in the machine, and
  the request-completion path carries the compiler's `Diagnostic` in the exit ring, so a
  compile error the service finds after `spawn` returned reaches `run`'s `diag` (`wait`
  still sees `Faulted(IoError)`). The `stdlib` is now real source: `stdlib/stdlib.ig` is
  embedded with `include_str!` and compiled into the same package as any application that
  says `uses stdlib;`, with library functions first in one function table, a shared
  constant pool, and line-table unit ids (0 app, 1 stdlib) so a broken library is a normal
  diagnostic naming `stdlib`. `function Name(...): T;` with `return` works for scalar
  results. The first helpers are `trim_into`, `split_once`, `parse_int_into`,
  `starts_with` and `contains_byte`; `samples/apps/text` exercises them on `main(args)`.
  Fixed-size arrays are implemented: `array[T, N]` (N a positive const) in state, locals,
  records, sum payloads and `value`/`const`/`var` parameters, with nominal
  element/count identity, `a[i]` read/write checked `index < N`
  (`fault(OutOfBounds)`), whole-array `copy_refs`, nested `a[i][j]`/`a[i].field`, and one
  new opcode `ref_element` (ABI v1 61 → 62, immediate `stride` and `count`). Layout is `N`
  elements at `align8(element size)`, array size `align8(N * stride)`; one structural
  array table is shared by the app and `stdlib`, so `stdlib.split_all` takes an
  `array[string[64], 8]` parameter. `samples/lang/array.ig` exercises the whole slice.
  Fixed-capacity rings are implemented too: `ring[T, N]` (N a positive const) in state,
  locals, records, sum payloads, arrays and `value`/`const`/`var` parameters, nominal in
  element and count. Layout is the `N` elements at `align8(element size)` followed by
  `head: u64` and `count: u64`; the built-in `core.ring_push`/`pop`/`peek`/`full`/`empty`
  are compiler-lowered over `ref_offset`/`ref_element`/`copy_refs`, `add_u`/`mod_u` and
  `eq`+`jz`, so no opcode was added (the ABI v1 opcode table is unchanged). `push` returns `Full` on a full
  ring; `pop`/`peek` return `NotFound` and leave the out-parameter untouched on an empty
  one; `push`/`pop` reject `const` receivers (a `value` copy is writable and private to
  the callee). One structural ring table is
  shared with `stdlib`, whose `drain_into` takes a `ring[int, 8]`; `samples/lang/ring.ig`
  covers FIFO order, wrap-around, copies and every element kind. Fixed-capacity maps are
  implemented too: `map[K, V, N]` (`N` a positive const, `K` one of `int`, `string[M]`,
  `bytes[M]`) is nominal in `(K, V, N)`, lays out a `count` header then `capacity` (the
  next power of two at least `2N`) slots of state/hash/key/value, and hashes with FNV-1a,
  probes linearly and deletes with backward shift. `core.map_put`/`get`/`has`/`remove`/
  `count` are five new opcodes (`MAP_PUT` … `MAP_COUNT`) so the algorithm lives once in
  the interpreter; `put` at `N` entries returns `Full`, a missing key is `NotFound`, and
  `get` leaves its out-parameter untouched. One structural map table is shared with
  `stdlib`, whose `map_get_or` takes a concrete map; `samples/lang/map.ig` covers
  collisions, backward shift, key 0, the empty buffer key, aggregate values, copies and
  the three parameter modes. UTF-8 is implemented: `core.substr_into` now accepts all four
  destination/source kind combinations, with the destination kind deciding the check —
  a `string` source keeps T4's code-point-boundary rule, a `bytes` source is validated as
  strict UTF-8 (no overlongs, surrogates, values above `U+10FFFF`, truncations or stray
  continuation bytes), and a `bytes` destination copies raw data. The source kind rides in
  the `SUBSTR` `b` field, so no opcode was added. `stdlib` gained `utf8_decode_into`
  (T3, the only checked bytes→string conversion), `utf8_len`, `utf8_decode` and
  `utf8_valid` as ordinary language code; `samples/lang/utf8.ig` covers every code-point
  length and malformed class and `run utf8` runs in the kernel. The checked slice stays in
  `core` permanently: the destination capacity must be a call-site immediate, and without
  generics a `stdlib` helper could only serve one capacity (the `stdlib.substr_into` move
  is withdrawn). Every online core now runs the scheduler: `smp::start()` brings the APs
  up with INIT-SIPI-SIPI through a trampoline copied to physical `0x8000` beside a
  parameter page at `0x9000`, one per-core TSS/IST set, one static 256 KiB kernel stack per
  AP (matching the boot core's guarded 256 KiB one, because any core may compile a
  request), and a bounded 100 ms wait, so `-smp 1`
  and `-smp 2` boot with fewer cores instead of hanging. Discovery is by attempt over APIC
  ids `1..MAX_CORES` with `MAX_CORES` = 8; the core index is internal and the APIC id is
  the identity software sees through `syslib.cpu_id()` (builtin 10). All cores run the
  same claim → quantum → release loop over the global runnable set, with per-core verifier
  scratch and per-core claim/yield/exit counters. An idle core does one `sti; hlt` step
  after checking the set; producers (`task::spawn`, `release`, `wake`, the REPL enqueue)
  set `need_resched` on one idle core and send the `0x40` IPI, whose handler only EOIs. A
  claimed task's slot stays reserved by task id and a reserved request id stays live while
  it compiles, so `core.wait` cannot race a spawn or a compile. The boot core's idle path
  prints the per-core summary, sets a stop flag, IPIs the APs, waits bounded for their
  acknowledgements and halts. `samples/apps/worker` and `cores` show six workers on
  distinct APIC ids in a `-smp 4` run; `xtask run` passes `-smp 4` by default (`--cores N`).
  The realtime class runs: a task promotes itself once with `core.realtime_on()` (builtin
  11; `Ok`, or `Full` when every online core already holds one), is claimed before normal
  tasks, is not released at quantum expiry, and is exempt from the total budget. It must
  call `core.heartbeat()` (builtin 12) at least once per second of non-blocked time; the
  deadline is `budget_ns + 1 s` (a compile-time constant) and a miss terminates it at the
  next quantum boundary with `Faulted(WatchdogTimeout)`. Admission is a CAS counter
  against `smp::online_count()`; a task releases its slot only at termination, so a blocked
  controller keeps it. Starvation is accepted (no reserved core). `samples/apps/realtime`
  promotes, beats through 200 control cycles and runs a `beat` child that reports `Full`
  on `-smp 1`; `samples/apps/spin` loops without a heartbeat and is killed after about a
  second. Streams now run: `in_stream[N]`/`out_stream[N]` are top-level state variables
  whose names are the stream identifiers, the compiler puts their 8-byte handles in the
  `state` prefix and lists the endpoints in the manifest, and the loader creates or pairs
  each endpoint before placing the task. A new endpoint whose peer is not live *waits*:
  a sender may fill it to capacity and a receiver blocks on it. A same-direction
  duplicate, a capacity mismatch against a live peer, and a declaration against a closed
  stream are load errors reported through `core.run`'s `diag`. `core.send`/`try_send`
  copy a whole buffer atomically (blocking, `WouldBlock`, `CapacityExceeded`, `Closed`);
  `core.recv`/`try_recv` fill a writable `bytes[N]` and return the count or a staged
  `-1`/`-2` (`string` destinations are rejected because a partial read could split a code
  point). Blocking uses `WaitFor::Stream(handle)`, a send wakes a receiver, a receive that
  frees space wakes a sender, and termination closes the owner's sides, discards queued
  bytes and frees the buffer at the close (the record stays until the surviving endpoint
  ends). `samples/apps/streams` (consumer
  `pipe_in`, producer `pipe_out`) proves in-order delivery through a blocking sender and
  the `Closed` exit; `streams_bad` proves both load errors. The `stdlib` framing layer
  now sits on top, as ordinary language code taking `const in_stream[1]`/`const
  out_stream[1]` parameters (a stream parameter's declared capacity is not checked, so one
  helper serves every capacity): `recv_exact`, `send_frame`/`recv_frame_into` (a `u32`
  little-endian length prefix), `send_line`/`recv_line_into` (`\n`, CRLF stripped, an
  overlong line keeps consuming to stay in sync), `send_u64`/`recv_u64` and `send_bytes`.
  Each helper publishes a whole value with one copy, so an error leaves the destination
  untouched; a receive is one byte wide because `core.recv` returns up to the destination
  capacity and there is no pushback. `pipe_out`/`pipe_in` now run through `send_line`/
  `recv_line_into`, and `samples/apps/frames` (`frame_in` + `frame_out`, with an ack
  stream so the producer exits only after the consumer drained) proves the length prefix,
  the typed `u64` view, both `CapacityExceeded` paths and the close. Every core's kernel
  stack is now 256 KiB: a compile on an AP overflowed the old 64 KiB stack into the
  neighbouring `.bss`. Stack guards are now complete (`kernel/src/guard.rs` over
  `arch/x86_64/src/paging.rs`): the boot stack, all seven AP `.bss` stacks and every task
  stack have a not-present 4 KiB frame. A cache of 32 4 KiB tables splits each 2 MiB page
  once and keeps the neighbouring pages present, so several guards share one table; a
  96-entry registry lets `#PF` print `stack guard: task N` / `ap core N` / `boot core`.
  Every PTE change is followed by a local CR3 reload and an IPI TLB shootdown to all online
  cores, because a frame may carry a stale translation from an earlier life on another
  core. A task stack is `stack_len + 4096` contiguous frames: the lowest frame is the
  guard, `regions.stack` starts one frame above it with exactly the manifest length, so the
  interpreter's `StackOverflow` check is unchanged; `reclaim` restores the guard and
  returns guard + stack as one unit on every destruction path. `samples/apps/deep` proves
  `run deep` reports `child: faulted StackOverflow`; a hardware guard hit is a hard backstop
  that halts with the guard named. Fixed-point float text now runs:
  `core.format_f64_into(dst, value, frac_digits) -> int` (9 fraction digits at most,
  half-away-from-zero rounding with carry, `nan`/`inf`/`-inf` specials, `-4` for a wider
  field or a magnitude at or above `2^63`, `-6` when the result does not fit) is one new
  `FORMAT_F64` opcode implemented as a pure buffer operation in the interpreter, and
  `stdlib.parse_f64_into(text, var value) -> Status` is ordinary language code accepting
  decimal text with an optional exponent plus the formatter's `inf`/`-inf`/`nan`, rejecting
  everything else with `Invalid` and saturating overflow/underflow to `±Inf`/`±0` with
  `Ok` while preserving `-0.0`. `samples/lang/f64text.ig` covers the whole surface on the
  host and `samples/apps/floats` proves `run floats 3.25` → `value=3.250` and
  `run floats nope` → `Invalid` in the kernel. Shortest round-trip float text now runs
  too: `core.format_f64_short_into(dst, value) -> int` writes the fewest significant
  digits that parse back to the same bits — positional while `-6 < e10 + 1 <= 21`,
  scientific otherwise (`1e20` vs `1e21`, `1e-6` vs `1e-7`), `0`/`-0`, `nan`, `inf`/`-inf`
  literal, an exact decimal tie away from zero, `-6` when the destination is too small —
  as one `FORMAT_F64_SHORT` opcode. It is an exact search, not an approximation: the value
  and both ends of its round-trip interval are expanded to base-`10^9` integers (at most
  769 digits, 88 limbs), the two `n`-digit decimals that bracket the value are tested in
  turn, and the first `n` that lands inside wins (17 digits always do). No allocation, no
  host call, no data tables beyond 72 bytes of `10^0..=10^8`. `samples/lang/f64short.ig`
  covers the shape boundaries and the subnormal tail on the host, and `samples/apps/floats`
  prints `s:1e21=1e21` and `s:tinysub=5e-324` in the kernel. The desktop milestone
  (M2) has started with its graphics foundation: the kernel keeps the multiboot2 mode in
  `kernel/src/gfx.rs` and allocates a full-screen **shadow screen** from the frame
  allocator (`pitch * height` bytes, 3 MiB / 768 frames at the 1024×768×32 mode GRUB
  returns) instead of drawing into the framebuffer directly. The shadow is the single
  source of truth; the text console paints its glyphs through it (`console_glyph` presents
  each cell) and `syslib` exposes nine primitives — `screen_width`/`screen_height`,
  `fill_rect`, `fill_dither` (50% `(X + Y)` checkerboard), `draw_rect` (1-px outline),
  `draw_text` (8×8 font, transparent background, no newline), `get_pixel`,
  `present` (pitch-aware row copy) and `clear` — as host builtins with `Status` results
  (`Invalid` for `w <= 0`, `h <= 0` or a colour above `0x00FFFFFF`; drawing is clipped,
  never faulted). A rectangular clip region (currently the whole screen) is the seam for
  the window service. `samples/apps/desktop` paints a System 7.5-style dither desktop, a
  menu bar with a right-aligned `core.clock()` HH:MM and a striped title-bar window, then
  checks nine pixels with `get_pixel` and prints `desktop: ok` in one claim. Graphics
  disable cleanly (no tag, non-32-bpp, bad geometry or no shadow memory: 0×0 screen,
  `Invalid`, console on serial). Spec 0009 and ADR 0020 record the decision; M2.2 is the
  mouse and events, M2.3 windows and `expose`. The desktop pointer now runs too: the
  i8042 auxiliary device is initialised at boot (`0xA8`, config aux interrupt cleared,
  `0xF6`, `0xF3 100`, `0xE8 3`, `0xE6`, `0xF4`; bounded waits) and polled in the service
  opportunity beside the keyboard, which now leaves aux bytes for it instead of
  discarding them. A pure 3-byte decoder requires bit 3 on the first byte (resync
  otherwise), drops an overflow packet whole and sign-extends the 9-bit deltas with the
  Y axis inverted; one machine-wide pointer state (`x`, `y`, buttons; clamped to the
  screen; 1:1 motion) lives in `kernel/src/gfx.rs`, and a boot self-check runs canned
  packets (including a negative delta, an overflow and a desynced stream) through the
  same decoder and clamp arithmetic on scratch state (`mouse: decoder ok`, halt on a
  mismatch). The 16×16 arrow (black outline, white fill, hotspot 0,0) is composited into
  the framebuffer by `present`, never stored in the shadow, so `get_pixel` stays
  uncomposited and headless checks stay deterministic; a move re-presents only the old
  and new cursor rectangles. `syslib` gained `pointer_x`/`pointer_y` (`int`),
  `pointer_buttons` (`uint`) and the debug-only `inject_pointer(dx, dy, buttons)`
  (builtins 22–25; host defaults 0/0/0 and `Invalid`), and `samples/apps/mouse` proves
  clamping at both edges, buttons and the "cursor is not in the shadow" rule (`mouse:
  ok`). Spec 0010 and ADR 0021 record the decision; M2.2.3 adds key events and M2.2.4 the
  event queue. The pointer is now **absolute** as well: `kernel/src/pci.rs` scans bus 0
  for the PIIX3 UHCI controller (`8086:7020` at 0:1.2, I/O BAR4), `kernel/src/usb.rs`
  resets the controller, installs a static 4 KiB frame list plus static QH/TDs and
  enumerates QEMU's `usb-tablet` (`GET_DESCRIPTOR`/`SET_ADDRESS`/`SET_CONFIGURATION`/
  `SET_IDLE`), verifying the HID report descriptor's absolute 16-bit X/Y shape. One
  self-linked interrupt-IN TD is polled from the service opportunity; the measured report
  is one buttons byte plus little-endian X and Y (`0..=32767`, top-left origin) and a
  wheel byte, scaled to `0..=w-1`/`0..=h-1` with round-to-nearest and applied through
  `gfx.set_pointer_absolute`. The tablet wins over PS/2 while ready; PS/2 motion is
  dropped (bytes still drained) and remains the fallback on a machine without the tablet.
  `syslib.pointer_source()` (builtin 26) reports 0 none / 1 PS/2 / 2 tablet. This fixes
  Q49's burst loss and host-window scale mismatch because an absolute report restates the
  position. `xtask run` passes `-usb -device usb-tablet` (`--no-tablet` omits the device);
  `scripts/tablet_qmp.py` drives QMP absolute injection and compares guest pixels. Spec
  0010 and ADR 0022 record the decision. The keyboard now
  produces events as well as console bytes (M2.2.3–M2.2.5): `arch/x86_64/src/keyboard.rs`
  decodes set 1 into a pure `KeyEvent` (stable key code, text, Shift/Ctrl/Alt/Caps,
  arrows, F1–F12, the E1 Pause sequence skipped whole) and a boot `keyboard: decoder ok`
  self-check; `kernel/src/event.rs` owns one 64-event machine-wide queue that drops the
  newest event on overflow and counts it, with pointer transitions enqueued from `gfx`.
  `EventKind` and `Event` (`kind, x, y, code, window`) are predeclared at
  `abi::layout::event`, key events pack `text | modifiers << 8 | key code << 16`
  (`abi::key`), and `syslib.next_event` (blocking on `WaitFor::Input`, SMP-safe wake),
  `poll_event`, `inject_event` (debug-only), `event_drops` and `event_flush` are builtins
  27–31 with no new opcode. `samples/apps/events` with `events_poke` proves the surface,
  the overflow policy and a genuinely blocked wake (`events: ok` on `-smp 4` and
  `-smp 1`). IRQ12 now drives the relative fallback too (M2.2.7): `arch/x86_64/src/ioapic.rs`
  routes IRQ12 through the I/O APIC at `0xFEC00000` (the page is split and marked
  `PWT | PCD`) to the boot core as a level-triggered vector `0x2C`; the `irq_aux` stub
  calls `mouse::on_irq`, which drains at most 64 aux bytes into a 1024-byte
  single-producer/single-consumer atomic ring and EOIs only the local APIC. `mouse::poll`
  decodes the ring in the service opportunity, and the direct controller drain remains
  only as the backstop when the route is unavailable; the two modes are exclusive, so an
  aux byte has one reader. `mouse::enable_irq` stops reporting, re-reads the
  configuration, re-enables reporting and writes `config | 0x02` last (once bit 1 is set
  the handler would steal the `0xF4` ACK); the configuration value is now written to the
  **data** port, fixing the old command-port write that left bit 1 clear. Boot lines
  `ioapic: version ...`, `mouse: aux IRQ12 enabled ...` and a one-time
  `mouse: aux IRQ12 delivered ...`; an absent IOAPIC/APIC or a failed write falls back to
  polling. `scripts/mouse_burst_qmp.py` clamps at x=0 and injects 300×(+2), requiring
  +600 on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`; QEMU 10.2.2 retains PS/2 deltas, so the polled build is
  also 600/600 headlessly (the historical 346/600 was a host-window measurement). Spec
  0010 and ADR 0023 record the decision. Spec 0011 (Windows) is accepted and fixes the
  window model before implementation (ADR 0024): a 16-entry kernel table of records with
  monotonic `int` ids (`0` invalid); `window_open(x, y, w, h, title, var id)` takes the
  frame origin and the **content** size (frame = content + a 1-px border and a 16-px title
  bar, so `frame = (w + 2, h + 17)`); one bottom-to-top z-order with exactly one focus
  (open/raise focus, closing the focused window falls back to the topmost live one, task
  death closes its windows); owner-only `close`/`move` with `raise` allowed to any task;
  a 64-byte title, an 8 × 8 minimum content and an origin clamped on screen; a
  service-owned shadow clip with save/narrow/restore (no application primitive); and a
  whole-content `Expose` repair protocol for M2.3.2/M2.3.3. Geometry is read through the
  predeclared `Rect` record (`x, y, w, h: int`, size 32). M2.3.1 implements it:
  `kernel/src/window.rs` holds the 16-entry table behind one lock (`open` clamps and
  assigns monotonic ids, `close`/`move` check the owner, `raise` refocuses, `close_task`
  runs from `Task::reclaim`, the rect/focus reads are pure); `syslib.window_*` are host
  builtins 32–38 with disabled-service defaults; `gfx` gained the internal
  `set_clip`/`reset_clip` (no application primitive) with a boot self-check
  (`graphics: clip ok`) and a scratch-table self-check prints `window: rules ok`.
  `samples/apps/windows` (with `windows_child`) proves geometry, raise/focus, move,
  non-owner refusal, close-on-exit, focus fallback and the error paths (`windows: ok` on
  `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`). M2.3.2 implements composition and repair: every mutation records
  bounded damage (32-rect desktop and chrome lists) and one synchronous repair under the
  table lock (window → graphics): the desktop returns as white + the `(X + Y)` black dither
  in the parts no live window covers, chrome is redrawn bottom-to-top in each window's
  visible parts (frame minus higher frames) intersected with the damage, and the union is
  presented once. The service draws the chrome (1-px black border, 16-px title bar — white
  with black stripes when focused, plain `0xCCCCCC` when not — an 11×11 close box and a
  centred 8×8 title) and never writes the content rectangle. `window: paint ok` checks the
  damage/union arithmetic on scratch state. `samples/apps/windows_demo` composes three
  overlapping windows, moves and raises them, holds 15 s as a realtime task and prints
  `windows_demo: ok`; `scripts/windows_screenshot_qmp.py` captures the frame via QMP
  `screendump`. M2.3.3 implements routing and `Expose`: producers leave `Event.window = 0`,
  and the kernel host routes at poll time — a pending owner-only `Expose` first
  (`EventKind.Expose`, ordinal 5, `window = id`, `x = y = code = 0`, one coalesced flag per
  damaged window), then a queued event, where a `PointerDown` hit-tests the topmost frame,
  raises/focuses it and names it, other pointer/key events name the focused window, and an
  injected non-zero `window` is passed through. Routing never holds the graphics lock
  before the window lock (the pointer producer runs under graphics and only enqueues); a
  repair marks exposes and the wrappers wake `WaitFor::Input` after dropping the window
  lock, with the scheduler's input-block re-check testing `window::has_expose`.
  `syslib.window_present` (builtin 39) presents a window's content clipped to its visible
  parts (content minus higher frames), owner-only; `syslib.present` stays a generic
  primitive. `window: route ok` checks the bookkeeping and `samples/apps/windows_events`
  proves routing, expose fields, foreign isolation, `window_present` and
  close-on-termination on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`. A client that ignores an expose leaves
  stale content and is not closed in M2. M2.3.4 separates the console and adds the first
  shell (spec 0012, ADR 0025): `console::init` opens two kernel-owned text windows
  (owner `0`) after `gfx::init` — a **terminal** for `core.write` (the application echoes
  its own input) and a **system log** for `println!` — each with its own 62×89 grid,
  cursor and per-window scroll, drawn at scale 1 inside the window's content and visible
  parts with an **explicit clip** (`gfx::draw_cell`/`present_within`), never the shadow's
  global clip, and presented cell by cell or part by part. `console::route_self_check`
  writes one probe through each real path, checks both grids and a foreground/background
  pixel in each with `get_pixel`, clears them and leaves `console: route ok`; every byte
  is still mirrored to serial and console input is the raw byte ring. A pending owner-0
  `Expose` is pulled by `console::pump` at the end of `console::poll_input` (service
  opportunity and idle loop), so the lock order stays `CONSOLE → WINDOW → SCREEN` and the
  repair never calls the console. `window::open` now admits owner `0` (task ids start at
  1) and kernel windows are ordinary windows: clickable, raiseable and enumerated. A
  boot-time `console: terminal ok` / `console: log ok` pair precedes the result line.
  `syslib.window_info(index, var id, var rect, var title[64])` is builtin 40 (no new
  opcode): index 0 is the topmost live window, `NotFound` past the end or with graphics
  disabled, and the compiler requires the title destination to hold 64 bytes.
  `samples/apps/desktop_shell` draws a System 7.5 menu bar (Apple square, File/Apps/Windows,
  right-aligned `HH:MM`, 22 rows) and a "Desktop" window; `File` lists windows through
  `window_info` into the terminal, `Apps` spawns `clock` with `core.spawn` (never
  `core.run`), `Windows` raises the terminal, and the Apple raises the shell. Check mode
  injects one pointer-down per item and prints `desktop_shell: ok`; normal mode promotes
  to realtime with heartbeats, paints, prints `desktop_shell: ready` and pumps
  `next_event`, and `scripts/desktop_screenshot_qmp.py` captures
  `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-desktop.png` via QMP `screendump`. There is no global work
  area yet: kernel windows start at `y = 26` and the shell window at `y = 72`; an app
  opening at `y = 0` covers the bar until the next shell event. A full-screen
  `syslib.clear` still erases the kernel windows' pixels (their grids survive and the
  next expose/output repaints them). The M2.3.5 two-application proof now runs:
  `samples/apps/windows_proof` plus `windows_proof_a`/`windows_proof_b` place two
  task-owned windows side by side, switch focus and raise by real injected
  `syslib.inject_pointer` clicks (the parent asserts focus and z-order because the
  machine-wide queue is shared), drive a four-step scripted owner `window_move` drag
  (old area restored to the underlying terminal, new content painted), then move B
  over A and back so A takes its owner-only whole-content `Expose`, repaints only its
  visible strip (no per-task clip) and repaints the uncovered content through
  `window_present` (the covered part shows B while B is on top), and finally close
  both windows and exit `Ok` on a stage-4 command acknowledged by `core.wait`. Two
  prerequisites the proof exposed are fixed: the claim scan starts at a rotating
  cursor, so a busy low-slot task no longer starves higher slots on `-smp 1` (the old
  scan always began at slot 0); and console cell/grid paints compare a window-service
  visible-geometry generation before and after and retry with fresh visible parts, so
  a window that moves above the console during a repaint is not overwritten. `run
  windows_proof` prints `windows_proof: ok` on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`; `windows_proof
  shot` holds the composed frame for `scripts/windows_proof_screenshot_qmp.py`
  (`/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-m23-proof.png`). Pointer-driven continuous dragging
  remains M2.4. The M2.4 language-level UI toolkit has started with its layout layer:
  `stdlib` now imports `core` and `syslib` (ADR 0026) and adds 18 ordinary-language
  helpers — 8×8 metrics (`ui_text_width` = code points × 8, `ui_text_height`,
  `ui_line_height`, `ui_content_inset`, `ui_title_bar_height`, `ui_close_box_size`,
  `ui_text_center_x`/`_y`), the System 7.5 palette as functions (`ui_black` 0x000000,
  `ui_white` 0xFFFFFF, `ui_gray` 0xCCCCCC, `ui_focus` 0xFFFFFF, `ui_accent` 0x000080),
  and client chrome (`ui_frame`, `ui_title_bar` with focused stripes/plain unfocused
  fill, an 11×11 close box and a code-point-safe 64-byte centred title, `ui_close_box`,
  `ui_separator`, `ui_center_text`). They draw only through the nine `syslib`
  primitives and never touch the window service's own chrome; there is no new opcode,
  builtin or `state`. `samples/apps/ui_layout` opens a real window, draws a synthetic
  dialog inside its content, presents it and checks stripes, borders, the close box, a
  button glyph and the accent swatch with `get_pixel` (`ui_layout: ok` on `-smp 4` and
  `-smp 1`); without graphics it checks the metrics and the disabled-screen `Invalid`
  path and prints `ui_layout: host ok` on `langhost`. The embedded library needed
  1,157,496 B of compile scratch at M2.4.0, so `MAX_ARENA_BYTES` rose from 1 to 2 MiB (the
  cap was provisional; spec 0008). String editing (M2.4.1) is implemented in `stdlib` as 13
  ordinary-language helpers with no new opcode, builtin or `state`: `str_boundary`,
  `str_cursor_clamp`/`left`/`right`/`home`/`end`, `str_insert_into`, `str_delete_into`,
  `str_backspace`, and `str_selection_normalize`/`all`/`collapse`/`replace`. Positions are
  byte offsets; insertion and deletion refuse a non-boundary with `Invalid`; a mutator
  stages into a local `bytes[256]`, converts through `core.substr_into` and publishes one
  whole-buffer copy, so a failure leaves the text untouched and the result is always valid
  UTF-8; the working capacity is 256 bytes (`CapacityExceeded` without a partial write).
  Cursors clamp instead of failing (negative → 0, past the end → the length, mid-code-point
  → that code point's first byte) and movement crosses whole code points; backspace at 0 is
  an `Ok` no-op; the selection is the scalar pair `start`/`stop`. `samples/apps/text_edit`
  exercises ASCII and 1-/2-/3-/4-byte code points, every rejection path, capacity and the
  selection helpers, prints `text_edit: ok` on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`, and produces the same
  transcript on `langhost`. Controls (M2.4.2) are implemented in `stdlib` as 32 more
  ordinary-language helpers with no new opcode, builtin or `state`: `ui_rect_hit`,
  `ui_focus_ring` (a dotted inset ring), `ui_focus_next` (wrapping), the packed-key
  decoders (`ui_key_text`/`modifiers`/`code` and `is_backspace`/`tab`/`enter`/`left`/
  `right`/`up`/`down`/`home`/`end`/`delete`, using `div`/`mod` because the language has no
  bitwise operators), a button (white or gray face, black border, centred cut label, inset
  focus ring), a checkbox (11×11 box, seven-pixel check mark, label, toggle), a text field
  (visible slice from a byte scroll offset, accent selection band with white text, caret,
  `ui_text_field_reveal`, never mutates the text), a vertical scrollbar (white track, gray
  thumb with a black outline, `ui_scrollbar_clamp` and a page/thumb hit class), and a list
  (`array[string[64], 8]` rows, accent selection, `ui_list_rows`/`hit`/`ensure_visible`/
  `move`). The application owns the event loop and every state scalar; the library has no
  widget object, callback, event pump or `state`. Drawing uses the M2.4.0 metrics/palette;
  too-small geometry is `Invalid`, disabled graphics draw nothing while the pure helpers
  keep working, and the focus ring is inset so it never leaves the control.
  `samples/apps/ui_controls` drives every control with injected pointer/key events and
  `get_pixel` checks on `-smp 4`, `-smp 1` and `langhost` (`ui_controls: host ok`). Menus
  (M2.4.3) are implemented as 15 more ordinary-language helpers with no new opcode,
  builtin or `state`: `ui_menu_bar`/`ui_menu_bar_hit` draw and hit-test a 22-px bar with an
  inverted active cell, `ui_menu_dropdown` draws a 16-px-row pull-down with a check column,
  right-aligned shortcut text, an accent highlight, gray disabled rows and `-` separator
  lines, and `ui_menu_bit`/`has`/`set` are `uint` bit masks (indices 0..63, `div`/`mod`
  only) that stay independent of the highlight. `ui_menu_selectable`, `ui_menu_first`,
  `ui_menu_open`, `ui_menu_close` and `ui_menu_move` are the state transitions: disabled
  and separator items are never highlighted, and `ui_menu_move` clamps without wrapping.
  `ui_menu_key` maps a packed `Event.code` to an action code (0 none, 1 up, 2 down, 3 left,
  4 right, 5 activate, 6 close): Escape closes, Enter activates, arrows move and Left/Right
  switch the open top-level menu. The application still owns the open menu, the highlight
  and the masks. The library is now 105,673 B of source, 79,824 B of code and needs
  2,966,496 B of compile scratch (128 KiB source cap, 4 MiB arena);
  `samples/apps/ui_menus` opens menus, skips a separator and a disabled item, toggles a
  check mark and closes with Escape using injected pointer/key events and `get_pixel`
  checks on `-smp 4`, `-smp 1` and `langhost` (`ui_menus: host ok`). Dialogs (M2.4.4) are
  implemented as 10 more ordinary-language helpers with no new opcode, builtin or `state`:
  `ui_message` word-wraps text in a fixed box (16-px lines, break at the last space,
  hard-break an unbroken run, `\n`, never split a code point), `ui_dialog_button_width`/
  `_left`/`_top` are the shared button-row geometry, `ui_dialog_button` is a button with
  the default action's solid double border, `ui_alert`/`ui_alert_hit` draw and hit-test a
  framed alert (focused title bar, wrapped message, default/cancel pair; slots 0 default,
  1 cancel; the close box cancels), `ui_input_dialog`/`ui_input_dialog_hit` draw a prompt,
  a `ui_text_field` over the caller's buffer and OK/Cancel (slots 0 field, 1 default,
  2 cancel; the close box cancels) and `ui_dialog_key` maps a packed `Event.code` to
  0 none / 1 default / 2 cancel / 3 focus next / 4 focus previous (Enter always takes the
  default, Escape cancels only with `has_cancel`, Tab/Shift+Tab move the focus). The
  application owns the modal loop, the focus slot, the result and the text buffer; the
  input dialog only draws and never mutates the buffer, and editing stays the M2.4.1
  helpers. `samples/apps/ui_dialogs` drives an alert and an input dialog with injected
  key/pointer events and `get_pixel` checks on `-smp 4`, `-smp 1` and `langhost`
  (`ui_dialogs: host ok`), and `scripts/ui_dialogs_screenshot_qmp.py` captures
  `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-dialogs.png`. Icons and the desktop interaction layer (M2.4.5)
  are implemented as 16 more ordinary-language helpers with no new opcode, builtin or
  `state`: `ui_icon` draws a 32×32 mono bitmap given as `array[uint, 32]` row masks (bit 0
  the left pixel, one `fill_rect` per maximal set-bit run) and `ui_icon_row_bit` reads a
  bit; `ui_icon_cell_x`/`_y`, `ui_icon_label_y` and `ui_icon_grid_hit` are the grid
  geometry (row-major, `count` valid cells, -1 outside); `ui_double_click` is a pure
  400 ms inclusive same-target test with no state; `ui_icon_grab`/`ui_icon_follow` carry
  the drag offset and position; `ui_selection_normalize` returns an inclusive band,
  `ui_selection_rect` the dotted outline and `ui_rect_intersects` the cell overlap;
  `ui_icon_drop` returns -2 over the trash, else the cell or -1, and `ui_trash_hit` is the
  named rectangle rule. The four bitmaps (folder, document, terminal, trash) live in the
  sample, and the app owns the cells, the live/selected flags, the click time/target and
  the clamp of a dragged icon to its content. `samples/apps/ui_icons` double-clicks the
  document (`ui_icons: open Document`), drags the folder to an empty cell and then into
  the trash, rubber-band selects the two remaining icons and checks the pixels and the
  band on `-smp 4`, `-smp 1` and `langhost` (`ui_icons: host ok`);
  `scripts/ui_icons_screenshot_qmp.py` captures `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-icons.png`.
  The M2.4 UI toolkit is complete with the widgets demo (M2.4.6):
  `samples/apps/widgets` is one 760×560 client panel and one event loop that composes an
  app-drawn File/Edit/View menu bar with a clock, a button, a checkbox, a text field
  (typing, accent selection, horizontal scroll and a 2-byte `é` through the M2.4.1
  helpers), a scrollbar over a 16-line document, an 8-row list, a folder/document icon
  strip with a trash target, an alert and a text-input dialog. The dialogs are the same
  state machine as the panel: while one is open every key and pointer event goes to it,
  Enter always takes the default, Escape cancels, and the input buffer is published into
  the field only on OK. Check mode drives injected pointer/key events and checks
  representative pixels with `get_pixel`, prints `widgets: ok` on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`
  and `widgets: host ok` on `langhost`; `langhost/tests/e2e.rs` pins the composed drawing
  sequence and the control/key results. It adds no helper, opcode, builtin or state (the
  library stays at 125 functions); the application source is 57,388 B and the pair needs
  6,546,928 B of compile scratch. `widgets shot` plus
  `scripts/widgets_screenshot_qmp.py` writes `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-widgets.png`.
  The application file API now runs (M2.5.1, spec 0013, ADR 0027): `core.fs_list`,
  `core.fs_read` and `core.fs_size` are host builtins 41–43 with no new opcode,
  exposing the application image to applications in that slice (the root-only view; spec 0018/0019 later added the tree and the writable `/home`). Paths use the shared
  `core.spawn` syntax (`abi::fs::validate_path`); in that slice listing accepted only
  `""`/`"."`, reads and sizes accepted one root component, and a separator was
  `Invalid` (subdirectories unsupported; spec 0018 later added the tree). `fs_list` returns entry `index` in directory order
  (kind `0` file / `1` directory / `2` other, inode size, at most a 64-byte name),
  `fs_read` copies at most 256 bytes at a byte offset into a compiler-checked
  `bytes[256]` (`Ok`/0 at EOF, destination length and `read` published together), and
  `fs_size` returns the inode size. Every failure leaves the out-parameters untouched;
  the kernel streams the ext2 blocks through new `Ext2::list_entry`/`read_at` with no
  allocation, `langhost` implements the same view over `samples/apps`, and
  `samples/apps/files` lists the root, reads `child` in chunks, checks offsets/EOF,
  missing names, a directory and unsupported paths, and prints `files: ok` on `-smp 4`
  and `-smp 1`. `mke2fs`'s `lost+found` is reported as kind 1 and refused by reads.

The first windowed application beyond the proofs is `samples/apps/clock_window` (M2.5.2):
one 260×280 content rectangle with a two-frame client bezel, an integer midpoint-circle
analog face, 60 minute ticks (every fifth an hour mark) and hour/minute/second hands,
plus a framed `HH:MM:SS` readout and caption. Geometry is integer-only and app-local: a
60-entry fixed-point direction table built once by rotating 6° per step (`DirScale`
16384), Bresenham 1×1 `fill_rect` lines, and no floating point, square root or new
`stdlib` helper (the library stays at 125 functions). `run clock_window check` draws a
fixed `10:08:30` and checks the bezel, the rim at 12/3/6/9, hour and minute marks, the
three hands, the centre dot, two empty face points and the digital glyphs with
`syslib.get_pixel`; `run clock_window shot` ticks live from `core.clock()` in a bounded
poll/redraw loop that never blocks in `next_event` (the scheduler preempts its spin) and
never calls `syslib.clear`, repaints the whole content on an owner `Expose`, and presents
once per complete frame with `window_present`. `langhost` covers the pure time
decomposition, zero-padded formatting and the direction table (`clock_window: host ok`);
`scripts/clock_window_screenshot_qmp.py` writes `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-clock.png`.
The app source is 17,744 B and its task package is 103,696 B of code (26 frames), 1,304 B
of state and a 20,896 B stack.

The first ordinary editing application now runs (M2.5.3): `samples/apps/editor` is one
640×480 client panel with an app-drawn 22-px File menu (Open, a disabled Save, Quit), a
path/read-only line, a 26-row text view, a scrollbar, a status/Ln-Col line and a caret and
selection band. The document is one `string[8192]` (valid UTF-8); `core.fs_size` checks the
size, `core.fs_read` fills a staged `bytes[8192]` in the API's 256-byte chunks and one
`core.substr_into` publishes it (a `bytes` source is the strict UTF-8 conversion), so a
missing name, a directory, a subdirectory path, a file over 8192 bytes or invalid UTF-8
reports a status line and leaves the old document byte-identical. The M2.4.1 mutators are
fixed at 256 bytes, so editing is one app-local atomic mutator (`ed_replace`: boundary
checks, prefix/insert/suffix staging in a local `bytes[8192]`, one publish, exact
`CapacityExceeded`) while the read-only `str_*` helpers work unchanged on the larger
buffer. The app owns the caret, scalar selection pair, first visible line and horizontal
byte offset; the view scans line starts on demand, keeps the caret visible, draws a
selection band and caret, and uses `ui_scrollbar`/`ui_scrollbar_hit` (track clicks page;
thumb drag is still deferred). Keys are Left/Right/Home/End/Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown,
Shift variants, Backspace/Delete/Enter/Tab (two spaces), printable text from
`ui_key_text`, Ctrl+A/O/Q and Ctrl+S (which only reports "read-only"); the File menu's
Save is disabled because there is no writable filesystem. Every frame is complete before
one `window_present`, an owner `Expose` repaints the whole content, the interactive loop
blocks in `next_event`, and `syslib.clear` is never called. `run editor check` loads
`child`, checks the line/addressing logic, every load error class, insert/delete/
backspace/Enter, a 2-byte code point, the 8192-byte capacity refusal, invalid UTF-8, the
read-only rule, vertical/horizontal scrolling, an injected `KeyDown` routed to its window,
and menu/caret/selection/scrollbar pixels (63 verifies) → `editor: ok` on `-smp 4` and
`-smp 1`; `langhost` runs the same load/edit/line logic without a screen → `editor: host
ok`; `run editor shot` prints `editor: ready` and holds 5 s, and
`scripts/editor_screenshot_qmp.py` writes `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-editor.png`
(1024×768, 30492 bytes). The source is 43,834 B, the task package 134,176 B of code (33
frames), 19,856 B of state and a 33,416 B stack, and the image root lists 54 entries. No
new helper, opcode or builtin (125 `stdlib` functions).

The application file browser now runs (M2.5.4): `samples/apps/browser` is one 680×480
window with a path/`entries`/`read-only` line, a 26-row app-drawn root listing (a
folder/file/other 8×8 marker, the name, the right-aligned inode size, an accent
selection band), a `ui_scrollbar`, a 2048-byte preview pane with line clipping and a
status line. `core.fs_list` is read from index 0 until `NotFound` into 64 fixed
`string[64]`/`uint` arrays; `.`/`..` are already excluded. In that slice `lost+found`
was listed as a folder (kind 1) and opening or launching it was refused because
M2.5.1 had no subdirectory traversal; a kind-2 entry is shown but not openable.
(M3.4 later added navigation and M6.3 create/remove; see the decisions table.) A
regular file preview is `core.fs_size` plus 256-byte `core.fs_read` chunks into a staged
`bytes[2048]` and one strict-UTF-8 `core.substr_into`, so a missing name, a directory,
an unsupported path, a file over the cap and invalid UTF-8 each set the status and leave
the old preview byte-identical. Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown/Home/End move the selection,
Enter and a double-click preview, Ctrl+L launches and Ctrl+Q quits; the view keeps the
selection visible and pages by clicking the track. Launch is a blocking launcher
(`core.spawn` then `core.wait`) of a regular source app; a folder or kind-2 entry is
refused, and the proof launches `editor check`, which terminates, so no child is left
running. Drawing is client content only (no `syslib.clear`), one `window_present` per
complete frame, and an owner `Expose` repaints the whole content. `run browser check`
lists, previews, checks the error classes, scrolling, a real double-click, an Enter and
pixels, then launches, and prints `browser: ok` on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`; `langhost` runs
the same listing/preview/selection logic → `browser: host ok`; `run browser shot` holds
for `scripts/browser_screenshot_qmp.py` → `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-browser.png`
(1024×768, 32847 bytes). The source is 33,970 B, the task package 121,688 B of code (30
frames), 10,832 B of state and a 22,608 B stack, and the image root lists 55 entries. No
new helper, opcode or builtin (125 `stdlib` functions). The terminal window (M2.5.5,
spec 0014, ADR 0028) now runs: `core.terminal_attach(window_id) -> Status` (host builtin
44, no opcode) binds the one machine-wide console terminal grid to a window owned by the
calling task; the kernel host supplies the task id and checks ownership, the first attach
releases the owner-0 boot Terminal, a repeated attach by the owner is `Ok`, another live
task is `Full`, and a disappeared window detaches to serial-only. `console::pump` pulls the
attached terminal's expose by id and the System Log's by owner `0`, follows a moved window
by generation, and keeps `CONSOLE → WINDOW → SCREEN`. `samples/apps/shell` opens a 560×360
`Terminal` window at `(24, 64)`, attaches before its banner and keeps the
`core.write`/`core.read_input` loop with application-side echo; `run terminal` reports the id, refuses an unknown id,
checks ink/background pixels and proves `Full` with `samples/apps/terminal_probe`, and
`run desktop_shell check` shows the terminal, menu bar and Desktop window together.
`scripts/terminal_screenshot_qmp.py` writes `/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-terminal.png`
(1024×768, 32228 bytes). M2.5.6 boots to the desktop: the image stages
`samples/apps/desktop_shell` under the file name `entrypoint` (the unit keeps its name;
only the load path differs), `repl::boot` tries `entrypoint` then `shell`, and an image
without the alias boots the text shell exactly as before. The boot desktop draws the
22-row menu bar with a direct `Terminal` item, opens its `Desktop` window, prints
`desktop_shell: ready`, promotes to realtime and pumps events; the `Terminal` item
`core.spawn`s `shell` (never `core.run`) and later clicks raise the live terminal window
instead of starting a second shell that the one-terminal rule would refuse, so the
desktop stays interactive while the shell owns the terminal. The desktop never attaches
the terminal itself. A graphics-disabled boot prints `desktop_shell: no graphics` and
spawns the text shell, keeping a serial REPL. Because the desktop is a permanent consumer
of the one machine-wide event queue, an application that also calls `next_event` competes
with it; the event-injecting regression suite therefore runs on
`cargo xtask image --shell` (no `entrypoint`, the pre-M2.5.6 boot), while the boot proof
uses `build/igis.iso`. `scripts/boot_desktop_qmp.py` boots the ISO without a manual
`run`, waits for `desktop_shell: ready`, clicks `Terminal` through QMP absolute pointer
events, types `terminal` over serial, checks `terminal: ok` and writes
`/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-boot-desktop.png` (1024×768); it runs on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`.
The desktop source is 17,289 B and its task package is 14,952 B of code (4 frames),
752 B of state and 1,136 B of stack; with `samples/apps/font` staged the desktop image
root lists 58 entries (57 staged sources plus `lost+found`) and the shell image 57. The
`font` task package is 11,296 B of code (3 frames), 264 B of state and 800 B of stack.
`stdlib` stays at 125 functions and
M2.5 is complete. The M2.6.1 proportional font now runs (spec 0015, ADR 0030):
`kernel/src/font.rs` holds the 95 printable-ASCII glyphs as 10-column ASCII art plus
explicit advances, and the first use renders a glyph into a fixed
`SpinLock<[Option<Glyph>; 95]>` cache of row bitmasks and ink bounds. The line box is
12 px (baseline row 9, caps 2–9, descenders 10–11); advances are variable (`i` 3, `m` 7,
`W` 10, `M` 11, digits tabular 6). Three host builtins (45–47) expose
`syslib.text_width_prop`, `syslib.text_height_prop` and `syslib.draw_text_prop`; every
byte outside `0x20..=0x7E` keeps an 8×8 cell with an 8-pixel advance (blank at or above
`0x80`), and with graphics disabled the metrics are 0 and drawing is `Invalid`. The
window-service title bar (truncated and centred by measured width) and the desktop
shell's menu bar/clock use the proportional face, with the menu hit ranges derived from
the label widths; `syslib.draw_text`, the console and every `stdlib.ui_*` helper stay
8×8. Boot prints `font: glyphs ok`; `run font` prints `font: ok` on `-smp 4` and
`-smp 1` and `font: host ok` on `langhost`, checking variable widths (`ii` < `MM`,
`Wi`), the `i`/`M` glyph pixels, the `é` fallback advance (16), the 8×8 path, clipping
and the prop service title. `scripts/font_screenshot_qmp.py` writes
`/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-font.png` (1024×768, 33080 bytes); `scripts/boot_desktop_qmp.py`
now clicks the moved `Terminal` item (x = 170) and refreshes
`igis-boot-desktop.png` (31481 bytes). The console's D3 paint now holds the window lock
(`window::paint_visible_parts`) instead of retrying on the generation, removing a
reproducible `desktop_shell check` chrome overwrite on `-smp 4`. **M2.6.2 completes the
performance slice** (spec 0016, ADR 0031): `kernel/src/window.rs` coalesces the two
bounded damage lists before painting (empty/contained dropped, touching/overlapping
merged; erase requests merge only within one coverage limit and a contained request with
a higher-or-equal container limit is dropped) and presents the coalesced rectangle set
instead of one bounding box of all damage, while expose marking still uses the exact
original damage. `window::damage_self_check` proves the rules and the coverage invariant
on scratch state and measures a realistic diagonal move: 4 rectangles / 510,874 px as one
bounding box become 2 rectangles / 55,348 px (89 % less present area); boot prints
`window: damage ok (present 4 -> 2 rects, 510874 -> 55348 px)`. `kernel/src/console.rs`
replaces the per-cell `present_within` with a bounded dirty rectangle: `write_bytes` and
`_print` present once per byte run, the line-discipline echo defers to one
`flush_pending` at the end of `poll_input`, and `repaint` presents each visible part once;
the flush re-reads the visible parts under `WINDOW` and copies the dirty rectangle from
the current shadow, and the lock order stays `CONSOLE → WINDOW → SCREEN`. The new
`samples/apps/perf` measures frame time and event latency with `core.clock()` (min/avg/max
plus the phase total; the 1 ms clock makes a single sample coarse, the average carries the
signal), first idle and then with `realtime` plus three `worker` children spawned
non-blocking and reaped with `core.wait`; every run delivered 200/200 injected events.
Measured on `build/igis-shell.iso` (representative runs; TCG timings vary by a few ms
between boots): `-smp 4` idle frames 60 total 18–27 ms (avg 300–450 µs, max 2–6 ms),
idle events 200 total 17–34 ms (avg 85–170 µs), load frames 60 total 109–121 ms (avg
1.8–2.0 ms, max 6–37 ms), load events 200 total 22–31 ms (avg 110–155 µs); `-smp 1`
idle frames 13–14 ms (avg 216–233 µs), idle events 21–27 ms (avg 105–135 µs), load
frames 175–177 ms (avg 2.9 ms, max 163–165 ms — the realtime controller is claimed
first and is not preempted), load events 19–23 ms (avg 95–115 µs). `stdlib` 125 functions, no cap change. **M2.6.3 completes
the visual-regression slice** (spec 0009 "Visual proof", G21; ADR 0032) and resolves
M2.1.2: `samples/apps/visual` opens a window whose frame is exactly the screen (content
`screen_w - 2` × `screen_h - 17` at `(0, 0)`), so the service chrome plus the specimen
cover both kernel text windows and every boot line. It has no clock, animation, event or
host input: it draws a fixed composition (focused prop title bar, prop and 8×8 font
specimens, a nine-colour swatch row, a `(X + Y)` dither patch, a framed box, a diagonal
raster and alternating bars), checks representative pixels, presents the content once,
prints `visual: ok` then `visual: ready`, and holds without drawing; the pointer stays at
`(0, 0)`. `scripts/visual_regression.py` boots `build/igis-shell.iso` headless, runs
`visual shot`, waits for the complete present, captures through QMP `screendump`, decodes
to RGB and compares exactly with `tests/golden/visual.png` + `tests/golden/visual.json`
(SHA-256 of the raw pixels and one hash per 64×64 tile). The golden
(`37802052…e9e81c9f`, 1024×768) matches on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`; a mismatch prints the
differing pixel count, bounding box, first pixel and tiles and exits 1; only `--update`
writes the golden; `--tamper` flips one captured pixel in memory and requires the compare
to reject it; `--expect-mismatch` inverts the result. No kernel serial dump or debug
builtin is added (the display surface already carries the presented framebuffer). No new
opcode, builtin, `syslib` primitive, kernel table or cap change; `stdlib` 125 functions.
**M2.6.4 completes the desktop milestone with the system menu** (spec 0017, ADR 0033):
`samples/apps/desktop_shell` adds a `System` menu-bar label after `Terminal` (measured
prop widths) and a pull-down built on the M2.4.3 menu model
(`stdlib.ui_menu_dropdown`/`ui_menu_hit`/`ui_menu_move`/`ui_menu_key`) whose items are
`About igis-v2` (`Ctrl+A`), a separator, `Restart` (`Ctrl+R`) and `Halt` (`Ctrl+H`).
`About` opens a modal desktop-owned window titled `About igis-v2` (content 420×200,
centred below the bar) drawn with `ui_message`/`ui_dialog_button`; Enter, Escape and a
routed click close it, and the service's damage/`Expose` protocol restores what it
covered. Closing the pull-down raises the topmost window under it (the console pump or
the owning app repaints) and then the desktop window, so the menu leaves no stale
pixels. The shortcuts are focus-scoped and modal-aware (they fire only when no menu or
About is open and the event is routed to the desktop or `0`), so the editor's `Ctrl+A`
is unaffected; `F10` opens/toggles the menu. `core.halt()`/`core.reboot()` are host
builtins 48/49 (no opcode; `Host` defaults `Invalid`): `halt_system` sets the shutdown
flag, wakes every core on the existing `0x40` vector where `wake_ipi` acknowledges
(`smp::note_stopped`) and halts even mid-task, waits bounded by the clock plus a spin
ceiling (the boot core's timer can stop first when the caller is an AP), writes
`system: N of M APs stopped` to serial and halts; `reboot` stops the other cores then drives
`0xCF9` (`0x02`/`0x06`) and the 8042 pulse reset, halting if the firmware ignores both.
`samples/apps/system_probe halt|reboot` is the probe; `scripts/system_control_qmp.py`
shows `3 of 3`/`0 of 0` APs stopped with no later serial reply and a second boot banner
after a reset on both core counts. `desktop_shell check` now opens the menu, checks the
accent/separator pixels, traverses with Up/Down (skipping the separator), closes with
Escape, reopens with `F10`, activates About with Enter, checks the panel pixels, closes
with Escape and a routed click, opens it with `Ctrl+A`, and verifies the desktop content
is restored and no restart/halt fired (`desktop_shell: ok` on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`).
`scripts/system_menu_qmp.py` opens the menu/About by QMP pointer events, captures
`/home/sinan/Desktop/igis-system-menu.png` (1024×768) and closes with Escape. No new
opcode (`stdlib` still 125 functions; the desktop now imports `stdlib`); the image root
grows by `system_probe` (desktop 61 entries, shell 60). The deferred Q50 scheduler bug is
fixed (spec 0007 Sch17, ADR 0034): the per-slot kill record is an id-tagged `AtomicU64`
(never reaches a later tenant), `request_kill` publishes under the slot lock, `task::block`
re-checks the record under that lock after the quantum-boundary check (the lost-wake
window), and `reclaim` clears only its own id. Boot prints `kill: records ok` (a self-check
that parks a record for a claimed scratch task and requires `block` to leave it runnable),
and `run kill_stress` spawns 300 blocking victims, kills each at a varying delay and waits:
`kill_stress: ok (300 iterations, 300 killed, 0 exited, ...)` on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`
(the same app hangs on its first iteration against the pre-fix kernel). Q41 is resolved:
`core.parse_f64_into` (builtin 50, pure interpreter code over `core`'s allocation-free,
correctly rounded `f64: FromStr`) parses decimal text half to even over the whole
mantissa, including subnormals; `stdlib.parse_f64_into` keeps its signature and delegates,
so `1e-7` and the smallest-normal boundary text now match the literal reader bit for bit.
`run f64parse` prints `f64parse: ok 52 cases` on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`. Q39 is
resolved: `core.poll_any` (builtin 51, non-blocking) and `core.next_any` (builtin 52,
blocking) inspect a `const array[uint, 8]` where source `0` is a console input byte being available,
`1` one input event (including a pending `Expose`) and any other value a stream handle
whose data or close is ready (a read select; send-space readiness is not included).
`core.stream_source(ep) -> uint` is compiler-lowered (`ref` + `load_ref`, no builtin) and
is the only way to name a stream in the array. One `abi::wait::WaitSet` (`Copy + Eq`, at
most eight sources) is both the interpreter's `WaitKind::Any` and the task's
`WaitFor::Any`; `wake_console_input`/`wake_input`/`wake_stream` wake a matching set, the
scheduler's post-block `task::wait_any_ready` recheck closes the failed-probe-to-block
window, and `stream::readable` is data-or-close on the caller's receive side. Readiness is
a snapshot, not a reservation: the following `read_input`/`next_event`/`recv` is
authoritative. `samples/apps/multiplex` (with `multiplex_sender`) waits on all three,
self-injects the input event and takes the console bytes typed by
`scripts/multiplex_qmp.py`; `run multiplex check` is deterministic and `run multiplex`
adds the console arm, both `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`. The image now stages 64 sources, so the
browser's listing capacity rose from 64 to 96. ABI v1 stays at 75 opcodes (two builtins,
no opcode) and `stdlib` stays at 125 functions. **M2 is complete.**
At the M2 close, **403 host tests**
(abi 22, compiler 158, interpreter 147, langhost 76, including doctests); ABI v1 stays
at 75 opcodes. M2 is complete; the deferred backlog and the accepted M2 limitations are
the next milestone's input. The current totals and milestone state are in the
"Current state" block above; this narrative is the historical record.

**M3 has started (spec 0018, ADR 0038).** The application image is now a real
directory tree: `lib` (importable modules), `base` (base applications), `home`
(user files, read-only) and `system` (kernel/boot applications), plus `mke2fs`'s
`lost+found`. The ext2 reader walks paths (8 components, 64-byte components, no
symlink following), the file API and `core.spawn`/`core.run` take hierarchical
paths, a launch name with no `/` resolves under `base/`, and the boot entry is
`system/entrypoint` (the `desktop_shell` copy) falling back to `base/shell`. The
samples moved to `samples/apps/base|system`; `xtask image` stages the tree. Boot
adds `ext2: tree ok`. The language side (`uses lib.math;` compiled as another
unit, `SourceReader`, unit ids 0/1/2.., 384 KiB/16 MiB caps) is M3.2 (done and
host-proven); M3.3 wires the reader into the kernel and proves `run module_demo`;
M3.4 gives the browser tree navigation. M3 is complete. **M4 is complete** (ADR 0039):
the predeclared nominal types and user-declared types (`<unit>.<Type>`,
`<unit>.<Type>.<Variant>`) cross unit boundaries, and the duplicated application
helpers now live in `lib/out` (`say`/`say_int`), `lib/inject` (pointer/key event
injection) and `lib/outcome` (`is_ok(ExitReason)`), with 40 applications migrated.
**M5 is complete:** the calculator (M5.1) and minesweeper (M5.2) are real windowed
applications over the existing UI/`/lib` surface. **M6 is complete:** M6.1 mounted a
dedicated 8 MiB ext2 home image from an ATA disk read-only through one path router
(`ext2w`); M6.2 added the writer and `core.fs_write`; M6.3 added
`remove`/`mkdir`/`rename` and the editor save; M6.4 proved reboot persistence and
closed M6: the system now has a real, persistent ext2 `/home`. M7.1 (done) adds
`core.fs_truncate` (builtin 57) for in-place shrink/zero-extend. M8.1 (done) makes
windows draggable by the title bar and posts an owner close request from the close box,
with no new opcode, builtin or language surface beyond the predeclared
`EventKind.Close` (spec 0011 W22–W25, ADR 0042/0043; M8.2 replaced M8.1's kill-on-close,
so a dialog such as the desktop's About panel closes itself and the task survives).
M8.3 (done) turns the `Apps` item into a real application-selection pull-down
(`Calculator`, `Minesweeper`, `Text Editor`, `File Browser`, `Clock`, `Widgets`, each a
non-blocking `core.spawn`) and adds a machine-wide **modal popup input grab**
(`syslib.popup_grab`/`popup_release`, builtins 58/59, no opcode) so an app-drawn menu
that lies over another window receives the click; both the Apps and System pull-downs take
it on open and release it on close, and F9/F10 toggle them. M8.4 (done) adds a
machine-wide **persistent pointer-region claim**
(`syslib.region_claim`/`region_release`, builtins 60/61, no opcode) and has the boot
desktop claim the 22-pixel menu bar: the press that opens a menu now reaches the desktop
even while another application is blocked in `next_event` (the M8.3 regression), a new
press inside the claim starts no service drag or close request, and the modal grab still
takes precedence while a pull-down is open. M8.5 (done) makes a dragged window's content
follow its frame: a topmost move copies the content rectangle to the new origin in the
shadow before the repair (`gfx::copy_rect`, kernel-internal, no `syslib` binding) and
records no new-frame desktop erase, so the snapshot survives; every windowed application
re-reads `window_content_rect` on an owner `Expose` and recomputes cached geometry before
drawing or hit-testing (spec 0011 W28, ADR 0046). `windows_drag` checks the copied mark
before its pump and a changed repaint marker at the new origin after it, and
`scripts/drag_content_qmp.py` drags Calculator, verifies the panel/display at the new
origin and requires a `7` click to change the display. A user-found File Browser drag
case is a follow-up (`4a0a3d4`): its `refresh_rect` now calls `compute_layout`, and
`scripts/browser_drag_qmp.py` proves the path/list/preview borders at the new origin with
the old absolute layout gone. M8.6 (done) makes queued input **window-addressed**
(spec 0010 I31 amended/I34, spec 0011 W29; ADR 0047): one kernel-internal
`window::input_target` rule gives an addressed record its window's owner, a window-less
`PointerDown` the region claimant or the topmost hit window's owner, moves/keys the
focused window's owner, and no target to an owner-0 kernel window, an empty desktop or
an unowned pending kind. `KernelHost::poll_event` peeks the head and a non-owner gets
`WouldBlock` without consuming, while the target takes the ordinary routing path (a
region press stays unchanged); the modal grab still takes precedence and skips the
target gate. `task::input_queue_ready` uses the same target for `input_ready`, the
post-block re-check and `next_any` source 1, and the boot self-check prints
`window: target ok`. This fixes the user-reported File Browser not reacting while the
boot desktop ran; `scripts/input_routing_qmp.py` proves Browser click/Enter navigation,
About's close box and a Calculator button with the desktop alive, and
`scripts/drag_content_qmp.py` no longer closes the desktop. `events`/`multiplex` own a
small window because window-less keys/moves belong to the focus. No opcode, builtin,
`Host`, `stdlib` or `Event` layout change (505 host tests; ABI v1 stays 75). M8.7 (done)
makes the shell's own `Desktop` window a help panel rather than the shell's lifetime:
its close box closes only that window, the desktop keeps its menu bar and launched
applications, and the Apple square reopens the window; losing it no longer exits the boot
entrypoint and halts the idle system (spec 0012 D12/D19; the close/reopen is proved by
`scripts/input_routing_qmp.py` with Browser and Calculator live). M8.8 (done) adds a
visible `Up` button to the File Browser path line (44×18, left of the writability label):
a pointer-down on it calls the same `go_up` as `Backspace`/`Ctrl+Up`, the status hint
names `Bksp up`, and `browser check`/`input_routing_qmp.py` prove it (the latter requires
the path to match the pre-navigation root exactly). M8.9 (done) makes the File Browser's
scrollbar thumb draggable: a press in the thumb records the grab offset, held moves map
the thumb top onto `top` across the travel (the selection stays visible), release ends
the drag, and track clicks still page; `browser check` and `input_routing_qmp.py` drag
the thumb to the bottom on both core counts. M9.1 (done) adds lexical syntax
highlighting to the text editor (`samples/apps/base/editor`): `base/`, `lib/`,
`system/` and `.ig` paths start coloured (keywords blue `0x000080`, type and
predeclared names dark red `0x800000`, strings dark green `0x008000`, comments gray
`0x808080`, numbers dark brown `0x804000`), other text files stay plain, and a checked
File > Highlight row toggles the view. `//` ends at its line, `{ ... }` block comments
span lines without nesting, strings stop at their line, the block state at the first
visible line is scanned line by line from byte 0 once per frame, and token runs use
whole UTF-8 code points through the existing checked slices. Selected text stays white
on the accent band; the all-black drawing path is unchanged when highlighting is off.
No opcode, builtin, `Host`, `stdlib`, ABI or file API change; `editor check` proves
every class, the spanning block comment, a block comment above the visible top line,
the plain-file rule, the menu check and the toggle-off absence of palette pixels; a
real QMP File > Highlight drag turns the palette pixel counts to 0 and back.
M9.2 (done) fixes the user-reported
`task 4: exit Faulted(BudgetExceeded) after 2893 claims (2891 yields) while
trying to scroll in the editor`: that was the normal-task 10 s CPU watchdog, not
a crash, because the interactive loop repainted the whole panel (with the M9.1
O(document) block scan) for every pointer event addressed to the editor, even a
no-op move. `handle_pointer` now returns whether visible state changed and the
interactive/shot loops repaint only on true (plain moves free; menu moves only
when the highlight row changes; thumb drags only when `top_line` changes; the
thumb press/release themselves free), the scrollbar thumb drags like the
browser's M8.9 (`scroll_dragging`/`scroll_grab_dy`, `scrollbar_thumb` matching
`stdlib.ui_scrollbar`, a page/drag clamps the caret into view), and the
block-comment state at every line start is cached in `bytes[8193]` and refreshed
once per document publication (`publish_staged`/`ed_replace`) instead of
rescanned from byte 0 each frame. `editor check` drags the thumb through the
injected pointer path; the new `scripts/editor_scroll_qmp.py` reproduces the
user's burst (3,000 no-button moves + a 300-step real thumb drag) and requires
`exit Ok` with no `BudgetExceeded`: the old code faults after 2,826 claims, the
new code exits `Ok` after 2,448–2,633 claims on `-smp 4`/`-smp 1` with the text
view scrolled and the thumb moved. The 10 s budget, scheduler and realtime
policy are unchanged.
M9.3 (done) adds a reusable `/lib` file-open dialog,
`samples/apps/lib/file_dialog.ig`, with one public function
`file_dialog.open_file(initial, chosen) -> Status`: it opens a task-owned
`Open File` window (520×380 content, centred), starts in the current document's
directory with its name selected, lists up to 96 image entries through
`core.fs_list` (directories/files selectable, kind-2 gray and refused), and
navigates with Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown/Home/End, Enter, Backspace/Ctrl+Up and
Escape, row click/double-click, visible `Up`/`Open`/`Cancel` buttons, scrollbar
paging and thumb dragging, and the close box (`EventKind.Close`). Only a visible
change repaints; an owner `Expose` re-reads the content rectangle. `Ok`
publishes the whole regular-file path into the caller's 256-byte destination
with one `core.substr_into`; a cancel is `Invalid` and a too-long result is
`CapacityExceeded`, both leaving the destination untouched, and a disabled
screen is `Invalid` without a window. The module imports `stdlib` (M5), owns no
`main`/top-level `var`/stream (M6), and adds no opcode, builtin, `Host` method,
file API or `stdlib` function. The editor's File > Open and Ctrl+O call it and
load the result through the existing `open_path`; a cancel leaves the document,
path, caret, selection, scroll and highlighting untouched. Its `PathCapacity`
is now 256 (`PATH_MAX`) and `TempCapacity` 260. `scripts/file_dialog_qmp.py`
proves the dialog chrome/face, the root→`lib`→`math.ig` navigation, `Open`
loading `lib/math.ig` (text 17,959 px, path ink bbox 79 → 86 px), Escape
cancelling with a 0-pixel text diff, and Ctrl+Q ending `Ok` on `-smp 4` and
`-smp 1`; the visual golden is unchanged.
M9.4 (done) raises the editor's document buffer from 8 KiB to 131072 B
(128 KiB, the spec 0008 per-unit source cap; `LineStateCapacity` 131073),
because the user could open `beat` (554 B) and `deep` (874 B) but not
`base/calculator` (32,516 B): the M9.3 dialog correctly returned the path and
`open_path` refused it with `too large (8192 max)`, leaving the old document
and a status line that was easy to miss. Every current source sample now fits
(the largest is `editor` at 81,509 B); a file over 131072 B is still refused by
size with `too large (131072 max)`, and an over-capacity edit still returns
`CapacityExceeded`. The editor state is 530,392 B and its task stack 174,328 B
(the `ed_replace` staging buffer is 128 KiB), both below the 1 MiB region caps;
`editor check` runs 2,120 claims on `-smp 4` (2,123 with the home disk) and
2,120 on `-smp 1` with no budget fault, loads `base/calculator`, verifies the
full 32,516 bytes and valid UTF-8, and restores `base/child`.
`scripts/file_dialog_qmp.py` now also selects the `base` calculator row and
`Open`s it: the dialog closes (40,199 px), the text changes by 27,137 px and
the path line becomes `base/calculator` (118 px bbox), then Escape cancels
(19 px text diff) and Ctrl+Q exits `Ok` after 1,353 claims on both core counts;
the harness holds a tablet click for 0.35 s so a busy dialog cannot lose the
USB press report. No kernel, opcode, builtin, `Host`, file-API, `stdlib` or
dialog change.

**M10 networking (2026-11-19).** The first slice (M10.1–M10.5, M10.8, M10.9) is
implemented: a legacy virtio-net driver and a polling e1000/e1000e driver behind
the kernel `net` facade, an allocation-free `net` crate (Ethernet, ARP, IPv4,
ICMP, UDP, stop-and-wait TCP client), builtins 62–68
(`core.net_config`/`net_info`/`net_mac`, `core.socket_open`/`send`/`recv`/`close`),
and the program-side `lib/socket.ig`, `lib/http.ig`, `lib/dns.ig`, `lib/dhcp.ig`
and `base/net_probe` units.
`net_probe http` fetches `http://10.0.2.2:6666/` over a real TCP connection and
prints `net_probe: ok` on `-smp 4`/`-smp 1`; `net_probe dns` resolves
`example.com` through `10.0.2.3`; `net_probe dhcp` obtains `10.0.2.15/24` from
slirp and then fetches the page. The same three proofs run over e1000 (`8086:100e`)
and e1000e (`8086:10d3`) selected by `xtask --net-device`; `--no-net` reports
`net_probe: no device`. `run net_echo` listens on TCP port 7 and echoes three
sequential host clients through `hostfwd`, proving the listener/`accept` path.
Sockets now block: `send`/`recv`/`accept` sleep on `WaitFor::Net(handle)` and
`net::service` wakes on data/space/EOF/close/timeout after dropping its lock;
`core.socket_timeout` sets a per-socket deadline (default 30 s, 0 = none) and
`WaitFor::Deadline` is a generic timer arm. `core.socket_source` feeds
`core.next_any`/`poll_any`; `base/net_select` proves the selector and 300 ms
recv/select timeouts. The DNS parser has a committed socket-free host check,
`samples/lang/dns_check.ig`, pinned by `langhost/tests/e2e.rs` (`dns_check: ok`).
**M10 is complete.**

**M10.6 NIC interrupts, the poll fallback and `net_stats` (2026-11-19).** The
NIC's PCI INTx line (QEMU GSI 11 at 00:03.0 for virtio-net and e1000/e1000e) is
routed through the I/O APIC to the boot core as vector `0x2D`
(`apic::NET_VECTOR`, `irq_net` stub, `net_irq` entry). The handler is bounded and
lock-free: it sets a pending flag, performs one read-to-clear device acknowledge
(virtio `ISR` / e1000 `ICR`, addressed through two atomics so it never touches
the network lock) and EOIs the local APIC. The used-ring drain and every
protocol step stay in `net::service`, which already runs before each claim and
in the idle loop, so an interrupt is a latency hint and polling remains the
fallback when the route is absent, when the boot asks for it, or when the device
refuses to unmask (ADR 0051). `ioapic::init` programs one redirection entry per
call and splits its page once, so IRQ12 and IRQ11 coexist. `xtask --net-irq
intx|poll` (default `intx`) selects the path by writing `igis.net_irq=poll` into
the multiboot2 command line, so the polled proof runs the same kernel and image.
Two device details are recorded: legacy virtio can leave the PCI INTx level
latched high from its boot-time test frame, so `net::enable_irq` reads the ISR
once after the route is installed; e1000 must mask every cause with `IMS` and
unmask only the receive causes (`RXT0`/`RXO`/`RXDMTO`/`LSC`) with `IMC` — the old
`IMC = u32::MAX` at reset actually unmasked everything, which polling hid.
`core.net_stats(var rx, var tx, var drops, var errors) -> Status` is builtin 73
