(no opcode) over the stack's `Stats`, and `base/net_perf` runs ten timed HTTP
gets and prints the counters before and after. Boot logs
`ioapic: version 0x20, IRQ11 -> vector 0x2d on apic 0 (level)`, `net: irq ok
(vector 0x2d)` and, once an interrupt arrives, `net: irq delivered N
interrupt(s)` (`net: polling (igis.net_irq=poll)` on the polled boot). On
`-smp 4`/`-smp 1`: `http`, `dns`, `dhcp` and `net_perf` pass over virtio,
e1000 and e1000e with INTx (103 `v=2d` deliveries counted with QEMU `-d int`)
and with `--net-irq poll`; `--no-net` reports `net_probe: no device` with no
hang. `net_perf` on `-smp 4` with INTx: `total 179 ms, min 16 ms, avg 17.9 ms,
max 21 ms`, counters `17/23 -> 77/113` (virtio) and `9/12 -> 69/102`
(e1000); polling gives the same counters and equivalent times.

## Decision index

| Decision | Record |
|----------|--------|
| Rust as the implementation language | ADR 0012 (Accepted) |
| x86_64 first, multi-arch layout from day one | ADR 0013 (Accepted) |
| All code runs at ring 0 in a single address space | ADR 0001 (Accepted) |
| Applications are interpreted source, not native binaries | ADR 0002 (Accepted) |
| Per-core interpreter loops; tasks claimed from a global set, never pinned | ADR 0011 (Accepted; supersedes pinning in 0003) |
| Cooperative safe points, quantum, total CPU budget | ADR 0003 + ADR 0011 |
| Static preallocated application memory; no malloc, no GC | ADR 0004 (Accepted) |
| Internal bytecode permitted; no JIT | ADR 0005 (Accepted) |
| MMU hardening: RO kernel text, guard pages, NX | ADR 0006 (Accepted, provisional) |
| Fully static placement; no runtime allocation of any kind | ADR 0007 (Accepted) |
| `fault(code, diag)` decides, `exit(reason, diag)` always tears down; one recovery, no retry | ADR 0010 (current; supersedes 0009) |
| Boot via GRUB + multiboot2 under QEMU, with own long-mode transition | ADR 0014 (Accepted) |
| Applications delivered on a read-only ext2 image over an in-memory block device | ADR 0015 (Accepted) |
| No capability model; every application may use every `syslib` primitive | vision R14 / spec 0001 |
| Framebuffer console with a bitmap font; no windowing | vision OQ 8 |
| Byte-FIFO streams, copied through the kernel, atomic sends | spec 0006 (Draft) |
| Pascal-like language, nominal types, no user generics | spec 0003 (Draft) |
| `core` (Rust) / `stdlib` (language) / `syslib` (hardware) split | specs 0002, 0003 |
| `bytes` (raw) and `string` (UTF-8) as distinct types | spec 0003 |
| One task per application instance; REPL-only in the first milestone | specs 0001, 0007 |
| Realtime class: no quantum preemption, one-second heartbeat, admission by core count, no reserved core | ADR 0016 (Accepted) |
| REPL is a kernel service; boot tries `entrypoint` (the desktop, M2.5.6) then `shell`; `run`/`spawn` carry `string[1024]` args through a 1024-slot queue and return global task ids | ADR 0017 (Accepted + addenda), ADR 0029, spec 0012 D19 |
| Console is a byte stream (no echo, no key events); one global nanosecond clock at 1 ms resolution, no TSC; compile diagnostics via a caller-owned `Diagnostic` out-parameter | specs 0003, 0005, 0007 |
| Console input: COM1 into a 512-byte raw ring, PS/2 into events only, non-blocking `core.read_input`; the application echoes and edits | spec 0005, ADR 0037, Q44 |
| Streams are single-producer/single-consumer; names are unique per application; framing helpers live in `stdlib` | spec 0006 (Draft) |
| Floating point in the first milestone: one `f64` type, IEEE-754, with a kernel-owned per-task `fpu` region | ADR 0018 (Accepted) |
| No visibility modifiers: every top-level declaration is public; conversions are `T(x)` casts; maps use FNV-1a with linear probing | specs 0003, 0004 |
| Boot: INIT-SIPI-SIPI through a sub-1 MiB trampoline, `MAX_CORES` static state; the framebuffer tag is optional (a missing or unsupported mode leaves the console on serial); one IPI vector | specs 0002, 0007, 0009 |
| ext2 subset: 1/4 KiB blocks, double indirect, regular root files, metadata validated; unsupported images refuse the mount | specs 0002, 0007 |
| `ExitReason.Killed`; dispatcher before every claim; admission counts cores online at call time; relative paths without `..` | specs 0003, 0005, 0007 |
| Bytecode: one package per application, 4-byte-word stack VM, split `compiler`/`interpreter` crates over `abi`, structural verification | spec 0008 (Draft) |
| Scheduler: slot table of 64 tasks, claim takes the value under a per-slot spin lock, 10 000-step quantum, 10 s / 200M-step budget | spec 0007, ADR 0011 |
| Delivery is source-only, permanently: the image never carries bytecode and packages live only in memory | ADR 0019 (Accepted) |
| Integer formatting is a staged `core` opcode; `syslib` ports are staged as scalar `uint`s | specs 0003, 0008; Q45 |
| Enums: nominal `type Name = (Variant, ...)`, variants are global ordinal constants in one 8-byte slot; `Status` predeclared; `EnumName(int)` faults `Overflow` out of range | spec 0003, spec 0008 (`enum_cast`) |
| `core.spawn`/`wait`/`kill` return `Status`; `spawn` takes `var diag: Diagnostic` and `wait` takes `var reason: ExitReason` | spec 0005, Q43 |
| `core.run` is spawn + wait with the pending id in the `Machine`; a failed request carries its `Diagnostic` in the exit ring for `run`, while `wait` sees `Faulted(IoError)` | specs 0003, 0005, 0007 |
| Compiler nesting limit 32; post-boot kernel stack is 256 KiB with a guard frame | Q42, spec 0002, `arch/x86_64/src/stack.rs` |
| Stack guards for boot, AP and task stacks: 32-table cache, 96-frame registry, CR3 + IPI TLB shootdown after every PTE change | ADR 0006, spec 0002 H2, spec 0004 C6, `kernel/src/guard.rs`, `arch/x86_64/src/paging.rs` |
| Task stack allocation is `stack_len + 1` frame: guard excluded from the region, present bit restored before free, reclaim returns guard + stack as one unit. `Region::allocate_guarded` enforces the geometry with `Region::layout_ok` (span = frames + 1, `start = base + 4096`, `len <= frames * 4096`, overflow-safe) and rejects a mismatch as `Status::Invalid`; boot self-check `stack: layout ok` | specs 0004 C6, 0002 H2, 0007 |
| Records: nominal, declaration-order layout with every field aligned to 8; `Diagnostic` predeclared at fixed `abi::layout` offsets | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| Sum types: nominal, 8-byte tag at 0 plus the largest payload at 8 (rounded); destination-driven construction; exhaustive `case` with by-value arm-local payload bindings; `ExitReason` is `Ok | Killed | Faulted(code: FaultCode)`; `FaultCode` variants are qualified | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| `stdlib` is embedded source (`stdlib/stdlib.ig`), compiled into the application's package; `stdlib.*` is qualified, the function table is library-first, line-table unit ids are 0 (app) / 1 (stdlib) | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| Functions: `function Name(...): T;` with `return value;`, scalar results only, `return` on every path | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| Arrays: `array[T, N]` is nominal in element type and count, `N` a positive const, index `0..N-1` faults `OutOfBounds`; element refs via `ref_element`, whole copies via `copy_refs` | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| Rings: `ring[T, N]` is nominal in element and count, elements then `head`/`count`, `core.ring_push`/`pop`/`peek`/`full`/`empty` lowered by the compiler with no opcode; `Full`/`NotFound` leave the ring and out-parameter untouched | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| Maps: `map[K, V, N]` is nominal in key, value and count; `count` then `capacity` slots of state/hash/key/value; FNV-1a, linear probing, backward-shift deletion; `core.map_put`/`get`/`has`/`remove`/`count` are five `map_*` opcodes and `put` at `N` is `Full` | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| UTF-8: `core.substr_into` has four kind combinations and the destination kind decides (boundaries for `string`, strict validation for `bytes`→`string`, raw for a `bytes` destination); the source kind is the `SUBSTR` `b` field, no new opcode; the checked slice stays in `core` forever (destination capacity is a call-site immediate) | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| APs come up by attempt: INIT-SIPI-SIPI, trampoline at `0x8000` with a parameter page at `0x9000`, `MAX_CORES` = 8, per-core TSS/IST and 256 KiB stacks (the compiler's bound, since any core may compile), bounded 100 ms wait; absent ids are not online | spec 0002 "SMP", spec 0007 |
| Every online core runs the scheduler loop over the global set; an idle core does one `sti; hlt` step and producers set `need_resched` before the `0x40` IPI, whose handler only EOIs | spec 0007, ADR 0011 |
| A claimed task's slot stays reserved by task id until reclaim; a request id stays live while it compiles; the boot core stops the APs and prints per-core totals | spec 0007 |
| `syslib.cpu_id()` (builtin 10) reports the APIC id of the executing core; the core index is internal | spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| Realtime: one-way `core.realtime_on()` (builtin 11) admitted by a CAS counter against online cores, `core.heartbeat()` (builtin 12) with a fixed 1 s non-blocked deadline, realtime-first claim, no quantum preemption, no total budget, `WatchdogTimeout`, admission released only at termination | spec 0007, ADR 0016 |
| Streams: top-level `in_stream[N]`/`out_stream[N]` state variables, handles in the `state` prefix in manifest order, dynamic load-time pairing with a waiting peer, same-direction/mismatch/closed load errors, four `stream_*` opcodes, `WaitFor::Stream` block/wake, kernel arena with first-fit free list, close-on-termination discards queued bytes | spec 0006 (amended), spec 0008 |
| Stream framing is `stdlib` code over `const ..._stream[1]` parameters: length-prefixed `u32` LE frames, `\n`-delimited lines with CRLF stripping, fixed-width `u64`, one-byte receives (a receive returns up to the destination capacity and has no pushback), one copy publishes a whole value so errors never leave a partial destination | spec 0003, spec 0006 |
| Float text is fixed-point plus shortest round-trip in the milestone: `format_f64` is one opcode (destination kind + capacity immediate), 0–9 fraction digits, half away from zero with carry, `nan`/`inf`/`-inf` literals, `-4` for a wider field or `\|value\| >= 2^63`, `-6` for a short destination; `format_f64_short` is one opcode with no width and no magnitude bound, the shortest digits that round-trip, positional for `-6 < e10 + 1 <= 21` and scientific otherwise, ties away from zero; `stdlib.parse_f64_into` is language code over a decimal grammar that saturates overflow/underflow to `±Inf`/`±0` | spec 0003, spec 0008; Q40/Q41 |
| Graphics: one full-screen shadow from the frame allocator, sized by the returned mode (32 bpp only); drawing goes to it and `present` blits pitch-aware rows; the console draws through it; a rectangular clip is the window-manager seam | spec 0009, ADR 0020 |
| The nine `syslib` graphics primitives are host builtins (no new opcode): `0x00RRGGBB`, top-left pixel origin, `Invalid` for `w <= 0`/`h <= 0`/colour above `0x00FFFFFF`, clipped never faulted, 8×8 font, `fill_dither` is `(X + Y)` even | spec 0009, spec 0003, spec 0005 |
| The window server is a kernel `syslib` service over the shadow, with `expose` repaint and no per-window canvas or user-space stream protocol | ADR 0020; M2.3 |
| Input is one machine-wide pointer (and later one event queue) owned by the kernel; the PS/2 aux device is polled beside the keyboard, the cursor is a present-time compositor overlay and never in the shadow, and injection is the debug-only deterministic test hook | spec 0010, ADR 0021 |
| `syslib.pointer_x`/`pointer_y` are `int`, `pointer_buttons` is a `uint` mask, and `inject_pointer(dx, dy, buttons)` returns `Invalid` above `0b111`; the four are host builtins with 0/0/0 and `Invalid` defaults (builtins 22–25) | spec 0010, spec 0003, spec 0005, spec 0008 |
| The software cursor is a 16×16 black-outline/white-fill arrow with hotspot `(0, 0)`, composited into the framebuffer by `present`, with a move re-presenting only the old and new cursor rectangles | spec 0010, spec 0009 |
| The primary pointer is an absolute USB HID tablet behind the PIIX3 UHCI controller (`kernel/src/pci.rs`, `kernel/src/usb.rs`): static frame list/QH/TDs, enumeration over control transfers, one self-linked polled interrupt-IN TD, HID `0..=32767` scaled to the screen with round-to-nearest, tablet-over-PS/2 precedence, `syslib.pointer_source()` (builtin 26, no new opcode); PS/2 remains the fallback and the driver moves behind `arch`/`platform` later | spec 0010, ADR 0022, ADR 0013 |
| Input events: predeclared `EventKind`/`Event` (40 bytes at `abi::layout::event`), one 64-event machine-wide FIFO with the newest event dropped on overflow and a monotonic drop counter, key events packing `text \| modifiers << 8 \| key code << 16` (`abi::key`), `syslib.next_event` blocking on `WaitFor::Input` with SMP-safe wake, and `inject_event`/`event_drops`/`event_flush` as builtins 27–31 (no new opcode); a key produces only an event, never a console byte | spec 0010, spec 0003, spec 0008 |
| PS/2 aux IRQ12: the I/O APIC at `0xFEC00000` (page split, `PWT \| PCD`) routes IRQ12 to the boot core as a level-triggered vector `0x2C`; the handler only drains ≤ 64 aux bytes into a 1024-byte SPSC atomic ring and EOIs the local APIC; `mouse::poll` decodes the ring in the service opportunity, with the direct controller drain as the fallback when the route is unavailable (modes exclusive) | spec 0010, spec 0002, ADR 0023 |
| Windows are records in a 16-entry kernel table: monotonic `int` ids (`0` invalid), `window_open` takes the frame origin and content size (1-px border, 16-px title bar), one bottom-to-top z-order with one focus and topmost fallback, owner-only `close`/`move` with any-task `raise`, close-on-task-termination through `Task::reclaim`, a service-owned shadow clip with save/narrow/restore, and whole-content `Expose` repair; implemented in M2.3.1 as builtins 32–38 with the predeclared `Rect` and no new opcode | spec 0011, ADR 0024 |
| Window composition and repair: bounded 32-rect desktop/chrome damage, visible-part subtraction (frame minus higher frames), one `present` of the damage union, white + `(X + Y)` dither desktop, service-drawn 1-px border / 16-px title bar / close box, synchronous repair under the window lock (window → graphics), content never kept by the service | spec 0011 |
| Event routing at poll time: producers never take the window lock; `Expose` is a per-window, owner-only coalesced flag (ordinal 5, whole content) returned before the queue; a pointer-down hit-tests and raises/focuses before delivery; `window_present` presents content ∩ visible parts; `syslib.present` stays generic; a client that never repaints is left with stale content in M2 | spec 0011, spec 0010 |
| Kernel text windows: `console::init` opens a terminal (`core.write`; the application echoes its input) and a system log (`println!`) as owner-`0` windows, each with its own grid/cursor/scroll; drawing uses an explicit clip (`gfx::draw_cell`/`present_within`), never the shadow's global clip; pending owner-0 exposes are pulled by `console::pump` in `console::poll_input`; lock order `CONSOLE → WINDOW → SCREEN`; serial mirror unchanged | spec 0012, spec 0009, ADR 0025 |
| Owner `0` is the kernel (task ids start at 1); kernel windows are ordinary windows (focus, hit-test, `Expose`, `window_present`) and the kernel calls the same public window functions | spec 0012, spec 0011 (W20) |
| The desktop shell is an application, the boot entry from M2.5.6: the image stages its source as `entrypoint`; a 22-row app-drawn System 7.5 menu bar with direct items (no dropdowns), `core.spawn` launches (never `core.run`), the File item lists windows, the Windows item raises the terminal, and the Terminal item spawns `shell` once then raises the live window; no global work area | spec 0012 D9–D11/D19, ADR 0025, ADR 0029 |
| The boot desktop is a permanent consumer of the one machine-wide event queue, so event-reading apps compete with it; `cargo xtask image --shell` omits the `entrypoint` alias and is the pre-M2.5.6 boot used by the event-injecting regression suite. The shell's `core.write`/`core.read_input` terminal is independent of the queue, though its prompt loop also consumes key events | spec 0010 I31, spec 0012 D19, ADR 0029 |
| Read-only window enumeration: `syslib.window_info(index, var id, var rect, var title[64])` (builtin 40, no new opcode), index 0 = topmost, `NotFound` past the end or disabled graphics, title capacity 64 compile-checked | spec 0011 (W19), spec 0012, spec 0008 |
| Fair scheduling: the claim scan starts at the slot after the last claimed one (round-robin over the runnable slots), so a busy low-slot task cannot starve higher slots on `-smp 1`; realtime-first claim is unchanged | spec 0007 (Sch12), M2.3.5 |
| Console stale-parts retry: every window mutation bumps a visible-geometry generation; a cell/grid paint reads it before computing visible parts and compares after painting, retrying with fresh parts (bounded) so a window that moves above the console mid-repaint is not overwritten | spec 0012 (D3), M2.3.5 |
| M2.3.5 proof: two task-owned windows; the parent-owned `Proof Control` frame origin is a persistent cross-task command stage (only `window_raise` is cross-task writable); injected pointer clicks are asserted by focus/z-order, not by which task receives the event; the drag is four scripted owner `window_move` calls; teardown is a stage-4 close/exit acknowledged by `core.wait`; no new service API or opcode | spec 0011 (M2.3.5), M2.3.5 |
| `stdlib` may import `syslib`: the embedded library is trusted source compiled into the package and an application already has every `syslib` primitive, so the UI layout layer draws through the nine graphics primitives with no new authority, opcode, builtin or cross-unit type | spec 0003 ("UI layout and chrome"), spec 0008, spec 0009 (G19), ADR 0026 |
| The UI layout layer is `stdlib` source: metrics as code points × 8, line height 16, inset 8, title bar 16, close box 11; palette `0x000000`/`0xFFFFFF`/`0xCCCCCC`/`0x000080`; `ui_frame`/`ui_title_bar`/`ui_close_box`/`ui_separator`/`ui_center_text` draw client chrome only, never the window service's frames | spec 0003, ADR 0026, M2.4.0 |
| Compile caps: 128 KiB of source per unit, 384 KiB of source per application and a 16 MiB compile arena (M3.2; the M2.4.6 combined 256 KiB application cap was raised). The M2.4.x UI/scratch growth history: M2.4.0 took the library past 1 MiB, M2.4.2 past 2 MiB, M2.4.3 to 2.72 MiB, M2.4.4 to 2.97 MiB, M2.4.5 to 4.13 MiB with a minimal app (114,823 B of source, 84,920 B of code; the `ui_icons` pair needs 5,386,584 B) and M2.4.6 to 6.55 MiB (the `widgets` pair, 57,388 B app) | spec 0008, spec 0018 M11, M3.2 |
| String editing is 13 `stdlib` helpers over the checked slice and byte indexing: byte-offset positions, boundary-checked insert/delete, a `bytes[256]` stage plus one whole-buffer publish (atomic and valid UTF-8), clamping cursors, `Ok` backspace at 0, and a scalar `start`/`stop` selection; no new opcode, builtin or `state` | spec 0003 (SE1–SE7), M2.4.1 |
| Controls are 32 `stdlib` helpers with no widget object, callback, event pump or `state`: the app owns the event loop and the state scalars; `ui_focus_ring` is a dotted inset ring, `ui_focus_next` wraps, button/checkbox/text-field/scrollbar/list drawing returns `Invalid` for too-small geometry and disabled graphics, the text field shows a scroll-offset slice with an accent selection and caret but never mutates the text, the scrollbar hit returns outside/page-up/page-down/thumb, and the list uses `array[string[64], 8]`; key decoding is `div`/`mod` over the packed `Event.code` | spec 0003 ("UI controls"), M2.4.2 |
| Menus are 15 `stdlib` helpers with no widget object, callback, pump or `state`: the app owns the open menu (`-1` closed), the highlight and per-menu masks; `uint` bit masks use `div`/`mod` (indices 0..63), checked and disabled are independent, separators are the label `-` and are never selectable, disabled items are gray and skipped, and `ui_menu_move` clamps without wrapping; `ui_menu_key` returns action codes 0 none / 1 up / 2 down / 3 left / 4 right / 5 activate / 6 close (Escape closes, Enter activates while open) | spec 0003 ("UI menus"), M2.4.3 |
| Dialogs are 10 `stdlib` helpers with no widget object, event pump or `state`: `ui_message` word-wraps in a fixed box; alert slots are 0 default / 1 cancel and input-dialog slots 0 field / 1 default / 2 cancel; the title-bar close box returns the cancel slot; `ui_dialog_key` returns 0 none / 1 default / 2 cancel / 3 focus next / 4 focus previous, with Enter always the default and Escape cancel only when `has_cancel`; the default button gets a solid double border and the focus ring stays inside it; the input dialog only draws the caller's buffer (editing stays the M2.4.1 helpers) | spec 0003 ("UI dialogs"), M2.4.4 |
| Icons are 16 `stdlib` helpers with no widget object, event pump or `state`: a 32×32 bitmap is `array[uint, 32]` row masks (row 0 top, bit 0 left), drawn one run per maximal set-bit run; the app keeps the assets; grid cells are caller geometry with a row-major hit test over `count` valid cells; double-click is same-target and at most 400 ms inclusive (the app remembers the time/target and a drag cancels it); drag is grab-offset plus pointer-follow with the app clamping to its content; a selection band is inclusive (`abs + 1`) and selects live icons whose cells intersect (touching edges excluded); `ui_icon_drop` returns -2 over the trash, the cell index or -1 | spec 0003 ("UI icons and desktop"), M2.4.5 |
| The M2.4.6 widgets demo is one app and one event loop (`samples/apps/widgets`): it composes the existing menu bar/pull-down, button, checkbox, text field, scrollbar, list, icon strip, alert and input dialog, owns every state scalar, and treats a dialog as a modal mode of the same loop (keys and pointer events go to it while it is open; Enter is always default, Escape cancels, and the input buffer reaches the field only on OK). Check mode injects pointer/key events and checks pixels; `widgets shot` holds the composed frame for the QMP harness. No new helper, opcode, builtin or `state`; the library stays at 125 functions | spec 0003 ("Standard library"), spec 0010, M2.4.6 |
| Application files are stateless `core` host builtins over one path router: read `fs_list`/`fs_read`/`fs_size` (41–43) and write `fs_write`/`fs_remove`/`fs_mkdir`/`fs_rename`/`fs_truncate` (53–57, `home/` only); no handle, paths are relative (≤256 B, ≤8 components), the application image is read-only and `/home` is a persistent ext2 disk | spec 0013, spec 0018, spec 0019, ADR 0027/0040/0041 |
| `/home` is a real, persistent ext2 filesystem on a dedicated 8 MiB ATA disk, written by a new `ext2w` crate behind one path router; `core.fs_write`/`fs_remove`/`fs_mkdir`/`fs_rename`/`fs_truncate` (53–57) are the only write primitives and only under `home/`; no journal, `e2fsck -fn` is the consistency proof (spec 0019, ADR 0040/0041, M6/M7.1) |
| The clock window is an ordinary windowed app with no new API: a 260×280 content, an integer midpoint-circle face and Bresenham hands driven by a built-once 6° fixed-point direction table, an `HH:MM:SS` readout, a fixed `10:08:30` pixel check, and a live `shot` mode that derives seconds from `core.clock()` in a bounded poll/redraw loop (no `next_event`, no `syslib.clear`), repaints the whole content on an owner `Expose`, and presents once per complete frame; the client never assumes a blank screen | spec 0003, spec 0010, spec 0011, M2.5.2 |
| The editor is an ordinary windowed app: one `string[131072]` document (M2.5.3 used 8192; M9.4 raised it to the 128 KiB per-unit source cap) loaded through `fs_size` + 256-byte `fs_read` chunks and one strict-UTF-8 `substr_into`, one app-local atomic mutator (`ed_replace`) because the `str_*` mutators stay at 256 bytes, and Save enabled (File menu and `Ctrl+S`): a `home/` document is written to `<target>.tmp` and renamed over it, a `base/` document is saved as `home/<basename>`; a failure leaves the document/path intact | spec 0003, spec 0013, spec 0019, M2.5.3/M6.3/M9.4 |
| The file browser is one windowed app that navigates one directory at a time (`Enter`/double-click descends, `Backspace`/`Ctrl+Up` truncates the path; 96-entry listing), previews a file at any depth through `fs_size` + 256-byte `fs_read` chunks and one strict-UTF-8 `substr_into`, launches a regular file by its full path (`spawn`+`wait`), and creates/removes entries under `home` with `N`/`Delete` | spec 0013, spec 0018, spec 0019, M3.4/M6.3 |
| The terminal is one machine-wide grid bound to a task-owned window: `core.terminal_attach(window_id) -> Status` (builtin 44, no opcode) with the kernel supplying the caller's task id; the first attach releases the owner-0 boot Terminal, a repeated attach by the owner is `Ok`, another live task is `Full`, and a vanished window detaches to serial-only. Exposes are pulled by id (terminal) and owner `0` (log); a moved window is followed by generation; the lock order stays `CONSOLE → WINDOW → SCREEN` and console input is the raw byte ring | spec 0014, ADR 0028, ADR 0037, M2.5.5 |
| The proportional font is a kernel-owned 12-pixel bitmap: 95 printable-ASCII glyphs as 10-column ASCII art plus explicit advances (`i` 3, `m` 7, `W` 10, `M` 11, tabular digits 6), rendered on first use into a fixed `SpinLock<[Option<Glyph>; 95]>` cache of row bitmasks/ink bounds. Line box 12, baseline row 9, caps 2–9, descenders 10–11. Builtins 45–47 (`text_width_prop` 1→1, `text_height_prop` 0→1, `draw_text_prop` 4→1), no opcode; bytes outside `0x20..=0x7E` advance 8 and keep an 8×8 cell (blank at `>= 0x80`); disabled graphics give 0/0/`Invalid`. `draw_text`, the console and `stdlib.ui_*` stay 8×8; the service title bar and desktop menu bar/clock use the prop face | spec 0015, ADR 0030, M2.6.1 |
| The console paints a cell or a whole grid with `window::paint_visible_parts`, which holds the window table lock across the paint (`CONSOLE → WINDOW → SCREEN`) instead of computing parts and retrying on the generation; a window opened/raised/moved afterwards runs its own repair above the console, and the generation still follows a moved terminal on the next pump | spec 0012 D3/D17, M2.6.1 |
| A repair coalesces its damage before painting: bounded greedy merge of overlapping/touching rectangles, contained dropped, erase requests merged only within one coverage limit (a contained request with a higher-or-equal container limit is redundant), exposes marked before coalescing, and the presentation is the coalesced rectangle set instead of one bounding box of all damage. The invariant is coverage of the original union (growth is safe because the extra area is copied from the current shadow); static 32-rect lists and a 64-rect presentation, no allocation, no new opcode or counter | spec 0011 W21, ADR 0031, M2.6.2 |
| Console cells are batched: a bounded dirty rectangle per terminal, one present per `write_bytes`/`_print` run, one flush per input service opportunity, and one present per visible part on a whole-grid repaint; the flush re-reads visible parts under `WINDOW` and copies the dirty rectangle from the current shadow, keeping `CONSOLE → WINDOW → SCREEN` | spec 0012 D3, ADR 0031, M2.6.2 |
| Performance evidence lives in an ordinary app, not a counter or threshold: `samples/apps/perf` reports min/avg/max frame time and event latency from `core.clock()` (1 ms tick; the average carries the sub-millisecond signal), idle and under `realtime` + three `worker` children, with 200/200 event delivery and every child reaped; the coalescing reduction is the `window: damage ok` boot measurement | spec 0016, M2.6.2 |
| Visual proof is a host-side whole-screen dump, not a kernel serial dump: `samples/apps/visual` draws a deterministic full-screen specimen (window frame exactly the screen, no clock/input, covers the kernel text windows, fixed pointer at `(0,0)`), `scripts/visual_regression.py` captures QMP `screendump` headless and compares decoded RGB exactly against `tests/golden/visual.png` + a SHA-256/64×64-tile manifest; the same golden matches on `-smp 4` and `-smp 1`, only `--update` writes it, and `--tamper`/`--expect-mismatch` prove the compare fails. Resolves M2.1.2; no new builtin, opcode or serial protocol | spec 0009 G21, ADR 0032, M2.6.3 |
| System control is two host builtins with **no capability check**: `core.halt`/`core.reboot` (48/49, no opcode) set the shutdown flag, wake every core on the existing `0x40` vector where `wake_ipi` acknowledges and halts even mid-task, then halt or drive `0xCF9`/the 8042 reset and halt if ignored; the wait is bounded by the clock plus a spin ceiling. The desktop's `System` pull-down (About/Restart/Halt) is app-drawn on the M2.4.3 menu model and closes by raising the covered window; About is a modal desktop-owned window using the damage/`Expose` repair; shortcuts (`F10`, `Ctrl+A`/`Ctrl+R`/`Ctrl+H`) are focus-scoped and suppressed while a menu or About is open, so `Ctrl+A` in another app is unaffected | spec 0017, ADR 0033, M2.6.4 |
| Kill requests are id-tagged per-slot records (`AtomicU64` target id, `0` none) resolved under the slot lock: `request_kill` re-reads `SLOT_ID` before storing, `task::block` re-checks and consumes its own record after the boundary check (the lost-wake window), `take_kill(slot, id)` and `reclaim` consume only their own id (ids are monotonic, so a record never reaches a later tenant), and a kill published before `release` costs at most one extra quantum; the boot self-check is `kill: records ok` and `samples/apps/kill_stress` is the proof | spec 0007 Sch17, ADR 0034, Q50 |
| Correctly rounded float parsing is `core.parse_f64_into(src, var value) -> Status` (builtin 50), pure interpreter code: spec 0003's grammar check plus `core`'s allocation-free `f64: FromStr` (round half to even over the whole mantissa, ties, subnormals, `±Inf`/`±0` saturation, `-0.0`/`-inf` sign), the bit pattern written only on `Ok`, one canonical quiet NaN for `nan`, and `stdlib.parse_f64_into` kept as a thin delegating wrapper so its signature and the 125-function table are unchanged; no opcode and no `Host` method | spec 0003 ("Floating-point text"), spec 0008 (builtin 50), ADR 0035, Q41 |
| Multi-source waits are two host builtins (51/52, no opcode): `core.poll_any` is non-blocking and `core.next_any` blocks on one `abi::wait::WaitSet` (`Copy + Eq`, eight sources); source `0` is a console input byte being available (refined by Q44, row below), `1` an input event (including `Expose`) and any other value a stream handle whose data or close is ready (a read select). `core.stream_source` is compiler-lowered; the wake path wakes a matching set, the post-block `task::wait_any_ready` recheck uses the same probes, and readiness is a snapshot with the following read authoritative. `Host` gains `console_byte_ready`/`input_ready`/`stream_readable` (default `false`). `samples/apps/multiplex` proves all three arms on `-smp 4`/`-smp 1` | spec 0003 ("Multi-source waits"), spec 0005, spec 0006 ("Multi-source read readiness"), spec 0007 ("Blocking and wake-up"), ADR 0036 + 0037, Q39/Q44 |
| Console input is a raw byte stream: COM1 drains into a 512-byte ring (newest byte dropped and counted when full, no echo), PS/2 key presses go only to the event queue, and the shell echoes/edits its own `string[256]` with `stdlib.str_*` | spec 0005 ("Console input"), ADR 0037, Q44 |
| `core.read_input(var dst: bytes[N]) -> int` reuses the retired `READ_LINE` opcode slot as `READ_INPUT` (`0x42`): a raw non-blocking read that publishes the destination length and returns the count, `0` when empty; a `string` destination is a compile error; ABI v1 stays at 75 | spec 0003 ("Console input"), spec 0008, ADR 0037 |
| Q39 source `0` is "a console input byte is available"; the probe is `Host::console_byte_ready`/`console::has_byte`, the block is `WaitFor::Any`, `WaitFor::Console`/`WaitKind::Console` are deleted, and `task::wake_console_input` wakes matching sets | spec 0003 ("Multi-source waits"), spec 0007 ("Blocking and wake-up"), ADR 0037 |
| Window drag and close are service-owned: a title-bar press raises/focuses and starts a grab-offset drag (shared `relocate` clamp/damage, public `window_move` stays owner-only), a close-box press posts an owner close request (owner 0 inert), and both run from `gfx` after the graphics unlock under one `POINTER_ORDER` lock; a handled press is suppressed before `event::push_pointer` publishes it (never enqueued, so no SMP poll race), and a second press mid-drag is consumed. M8.1's kill-on-close is superseded by M8.2 | spec 0011 W22–W24, ADR 0042, M8.1 |
| A close-box press is an owner close request, never a task kill: a per-window `close_pending` flag is served as `EventKind.Close` (ordinal 6, `window = id`) through `poll_event` before `Expose` and before the queue, owner-only and topmost-first (`take_close`/`has_close`); it counts as input readiness (`input_ready`, the post-block `Input` re-check, `next_any` source 1) and the pointer hook wakes after the unlock. The window stays until its owner closes it; an ignored request leaves it. `desktop_shell` closes only About and stays alive; user apps close their own window; proof/demo windows may ignore it | spec 0011 W23/W25, spec 0010 I19/I31, spec 0003, ADR 0043 (amends 0042), M8.2 |
| An app-drawn pull-down needs a machine-wide modal popup input grab: `syslib.popup_grab(x,y,w,h)` (58) / `popup_release()` (59), one `Grab { owner, rect }` in the window table, `Ok`/`Full`/`Invalid` and owner-only release; while held `pointer_event` ignores presses and `poll_event` gives non-owners `WouldBlock` without consuming the queue while the owner receives producer events unchanged (`window = 0`, no hit-test/raise); `input_ready`, the post-block re-check and `next_any` source 1 use `grab_allows(task)`; release and `close_task` wake input waiters. The desktop's Apps (Calculator, Minesweeper, Text Editor, File Browser, Clock, Widgets) and System menus take it on open; a row over another window is now reachable | spec 0011 W26, spec 0010 I19/I31, spec 0012 D10/D11/D20, spec 0003, spec 0008 (58/59), ADR 0044, M8.3 |
| An app-drawn menu bar needs a persistent pointer-region claim: `syslib.region_claim(x,y,w,h)` (60) / `region_release()` (61), one `Region { owner, rect }` beside the grab, `Ok`/`Full`/`Invalid` and owner-only release; while held (and no grab is active) a queued `PointerDown` with `window = 0` inside the rect is dequeued only by the owner (non-owners get `WouldBlock` without consuming it), the modal grab still takes precedence, `pointer_event` starts no drag and posts no close request for a new press inside it while moves/releases still follow a drag, `task::input_queue_ready` folds the rule into queue readiness, and release/`close_task` wake input waiters. The desktop claims `(0, 0, screen_w, 22)` at startup and releases on exit; the bar click reaches it while a client is blocked in `next_event` | spec 0011 W27, spec 0010 I19/I31, spec 0012 D20, spec 0003, spec 0008 (60/61), ADR 0045, M8.4 |
| A topmost window move copies its content rectangle to the new origin in the shadow before the repair (internal `gfx::copy_rect`, overlap-safe, clipped, no application binding) and omits the new-frame desktop erase so the snapshot survives; non-topmost moves keep both erases and rely on the client. Every windowed app re-reads `window_content_rect` on an owner `Expose` and recomputes cached geometry, because drawing and hit-testing use absolute screen coordinates. The copy is a snapshot, not a canvas. A user-found Browser case exposed a missing `compute_layout` in `refresh_rect`; follow-up `4a0a3d4` calls it and `scripts/browser_drag_qmp.py` proves the new path/list/preview borders and the old layout gone | spec 0011 W12/W17 clarified + W28, spec 0012 D12/D19, ADR 0046, M8.5 |
| Queued input is window-addressed: one kernel-internal `window::input_target` rule decides who may dequeue (an addressed record its window's owner; a window-less `PointerDown` the region claimant or the topmost hit window's owner; moves/keys the focused window's owner; owner `0`/empty/unknown has no target and keeps the first-poller competition). `poll_event` peeks without consuming and a non-owner gets `WouldBlock`; the owner takes the ordinary routing path and a region press stays unchanged; the modal grab takes precedence and skips the target gate. `task::input_queue_ready` uses the same target for readiness, the post-block re-check and `next_any` source 1. The queue is still one FIFO, so an unresponsive target can head-of-line block later records (W18); `Close`/`Expose` stay owner-only flags. `window: target ok` is the boot self-check; `scripts/input_routing_qmp.py` proves Browser click/Enter, About's close box and a Calculator click with the desktop alive, and `drag_content_qmp.py` no longer closes the desktop. `events`/`multiplex` own a small window because window-less keys/moves belong to the focus. No opcode, builtin, `Host`, `stdlib` or `Event` layout change | spec 0010 I31 amended + I34, spec 0011 W29, spec 0012 D19, ADR 0047, M8.6 |
| The shell's own `Desktop` window is a help panel, not the shell's lifetime: its close box closes only that window, the desktop keeps running and the Apple square reopens it, so a boot desktop cannot halt the system by losing its own window | spec 0012 D12/D19, M8.7 |
| The file browser's path line carries a visible 44×18 `Up` button (left of the writability label) that calls the same `go_up` as `Backspace`/`Ctrl+Up`; `compute_layout` owns its geometry and the status hint names `Bksp up` | spec 0013, M8.8 |
| The file browser's scrollbar thumb is draggable: a press (hit class 3) records the offset, held moves map the thumb top onto `top` over the travel with the selection kept visible, release ends it; track clicks still page. The thumb arithmetic matches `stdlib.ui_scrollbar` | spec 0013, M8.9 |
| The text editor highlights source lexically, app-locally: `base/`/`lib/`/`system/` or `.ig` paths start on, plain text files off, and a checked File > Highlight row (five-row menu) toggles it. One line is scanned into keyword/type/string/comment/number/default runs (palette `0x000080`/`0x800000`/`0x008000`/`0x808080`/`0x804000`); `//` ends at its line, `{ ... }` spans lines without nesting, strings stop at their line, the block state at the first visible line was computed line by line from byte 0 once per frame (M9.2 caches it per line start), and whole code points plus the checked slices keep UTF-8 intact. Selected text stays white; highlighting off is the old all-black path. No opcode, builtin, `Host`, `stdlib` or API change | spec 0013, M9.1 |
| The editor repaints a pointer event only when it changes visible state (M9.2): plain moves over the text and same-row menu moves are free, a thumb drag repaints only when `top_line` changes, and the thumb press/release themselves are free; key events and `Expose` repaint as before. The thumb drags like the browser's M8.9 (`scroll_dragging`/`scroll_grab_dy`, geometry matching `stdlib.ui_scrollbar`, track clicks still page, the caret is clamped into view), and the block-comment state at every line start is cached in `bytes[8193]` and refreshed once per publication (`publish_staged`/`ed_replace`), so a frame scans only the visible lines (M9.4 raises this to `bytes[131073]` with the 128 KiB buffer). The reported `BudgetExceeded` after 2,893 claims was the normal 10 s CPU watchdog caused by a full repaint per pointer move, not a crash; the budget, scheduler and realtime policy are unchanged. `editor check` drags the thumb through the injected path and `scripts/editor_scroll_qmp.py` proves the burst ends `Ok` (old: fault at 2,826 claims; new: `Ok` at 2,448–2,633). No opcode, builtin, `Host` or `stdlib` change | spec 0013, M9.2 |
| A reusable file-open dialog is a `/lib` module, not a kernel service (M9.3): `samples/apps/lib/file_dialog.ig` exports `open_file(initial, var chosen) -> Status`, imports `stdlib` under the M5 rule, owns no state under M6, and opens a normal task-owned `Open File` window. It starts in the initial path's directory, lists up to 96 entries through `core.fs_list` (kind-2 gray/refused), navigates by keys, row click/double-click, `Up`/`Open`/`Cancel`, scrollbar paging/thumb dragging and the owner close request, repaints only on visible change, and publishes a whole regular-file path only on `Ok`; cancel is `Invalid` and a too-long result `CapacityExceeded` with the destination untouched, and a disabled screen is `Invalid`. The editor's File > Open/Ctrl+O call it and load through `open_path` (`PathCapacity` 256, `TempCapacity` 260). No opcode, builtin, `Host`, `stdlib` or file API change | spec 0013, spec 0018, M9.3 |
| The editor's document capacity is an application constant: 131072 B (128 KiB, the spec 0008 per-unit source cap) from M9.4, with `LineStateCapacity` 131073 and a 128 KiB `ed_replace` staging buffer; a file over the cap is refused by size with `too large (131072 max)` and an over-capacity edit returns `CapacityExceeded`. State is 530,392 B and stack 174,328 B (both under the 1 MiB region caps). The `/lib` dialog stays generic and does not filter by size; the reported `base/calculator` (32,516 B) failure was the editor's 8 KiB refusal after the dialog returned the path | spec 0003 ("Application buffers above 256 bytes"), spec 0013 ("The text editor"), M9.4 |
| The IPv4 wire stack (Ethernet, ARP, ICMP, UDP, minimal TCP) lives in an allocation-free `net` crate used by the kernel; the application surface is the `samples/apps/lib/socket.ig` unit on the program/interpreter side, and DNS/HTTP are ordinary `/lib` source. Host builtins cross the boundary as opaque handles, never addresses; the wire code is host-testable against a simulated link | spec 0020, ADR 0048 |
| The first NIC is virtio-net (legacy/transitional I/O BAR0, static virtqueues and identity-mapped DMA, no offloads) behind one `NetDevice` seam; polling is first and INTx vector `0x2D` is added in M10.6. e1000/e1000e follows behind the same seam (VMware exposes `e1000`/`e1000e`/`VMXNET3`, not virtio-net); modern virtio, RTL8139 and a userspace driver are rejected for M10 | spec 0020, ADR 0049 |
| Sockets are owner-checked opaque handles `(generation << 8) \| index` in a fixed table, closed on task termination; the ABI is `core.net_*`/`core.socket_*` builtins 62–73 (no opcode), blocking uses `WaitFor::Net(handle)` and a new `WaitFor::Deadline(ns)`, and `core.socket_source` tags a `next_any` source as `1 << 63 \| handle`. IPv4 is a `uint` in host order, ports are `uint`, and no new predeclared record is added | spec 0020, ADR 0050 |
| The first M10 socket slice is non-blocking: `socket_open` returns at once and the kernel `net::service()` drives the handshake; `send`/`recv` return `WouldBlock` and the caller retries under a `core.clock()` deadline. `core.socket_recv` stages the destination's declared `bytes[N]` capacity as a hidden compiler scalar (at most 2048 bytes per call) because a buffer header holds only the runtime length. `WaitFor::Net`/`Deadline`, `socket_source` and the blocking form are the follow-up; the kernel service closes owned sockets on `Task::reclaim` | spec 0020 N13 staging, spec 0005, M10.3/M10.4 (`e08745d`) |
| The blocking follow-up now exists: `WaitFor::Net(handle)` and `WaitFor::Deadline(ns)`; a host `WouldBlock` makes the machine return `Outcome::Blocked` and retry the same instruction after the wake. `net::service` expires per-socket deadlines, compares a readiness event sequence and calls `task::wake_net` after dropping `NET`; the post-block probe uses the recorded direction, so a lost wake is not possible. `core.socket_timeout` (71) sets a per-socket deadline (0 = none, default 30 s; the TCP connect keeps its 5 s `Closed`). `core.socket_source` is compiler-lowered to `1 << 63 \| handle`, and `next_any`/`poll_any` use `Host::socket_readable`; `net_select` proves the selector and 300 ms recv/select timeouts | spec 0020 N13, spec 0005, ADR 0036/0050, M10.3/M10.4 blocking (`5ae91bc`) |
| DHCP is ordinary `/lib` source over the UDP socket unit (`lib/dhcp.ig`): DISCOVER/OFFER/REQUEST/ACK from local port 68 to the limited broadcast, options 1/3/6/50/53/54/55/51, a 6 s cap and one NAK restart; `net_probe dhcp` applies the lease through `core.net_config`. A broadcast send is allowed before an address is configured, and a socket opened to the broadcast address accepts the server's reply from any source. No renewal, INFORM/RELEASE or authentication; the transaction id and magic cookie are the guard | spec 0020 N8/N18, M10.8 (`3752d17`) |
| e1000/82540EM (`8086:100e`) and e1000e/82574L (`8086:10d3`) sit behind the same `NetDevice` seam as virtio-net: MMIO BAR0 mapped uncached, legacy 16-byte descriptors, 32 RX + 16 TX static 2 KiB buffers, a monotonic TX cursor and RX tail give-back, memory-space + bus-master enable. `xtask --net-device virtio\|e1000\|e1000e` selects the device; virtio remains the default. This is the VMware/real-hardware path; VMXNET3 remains future work | spec 0020 N1–N4, ADR 0049, M10.9 (`c7989c4`) |
| A TCP listener is `KIND_LISTEN` (open kind 3, nonzero local bind port) with a two-handle pending queue: an inbound SYN creates a `SynReceived` connection and answers SYN-ACK, the final ACK moves it to `Established` and enqueues it, and `core.socket_accept` (72) returns the oldest as a normal established handle. A third completed connection gets RST; a listener close/owner death releases the listener, its pending connections and its half-open slots. `lib/socket.listen/accept` and `samples/apps/base/net_echo` (three host clients through `hostfwd`) are the proof | spec 0020 N10, M10.7 (`efeeadf`) |
| The NIC takes one PCI INTx line (QEMU GSI 11 at 00:03.0) through the I/O APIC to the boot core as vector `0x2D`. The handler is bounded and lock-free: a pending flag, one read-to-clear device acknowledge (virtio `ISR` / e1000 `ICR`, addressed through atomics so it takes no network lock) and an EOI; the used-ring drain stays in `net::service`, so an interrupt is a hint and polling is the fallback (route absent, boot-time `--net-irq poll` via the multiboot2 command line `igis.net_irq=poll`, or a device that refuses to unmask). `ioapic::init` programs one redirection entry per call and splits its page once, so IRQ12 and IRQ11 coexist. Legacy virtio can latch its INTx level high from the boot test frame, so `net::enable_irq` reads the ISR once after the route; e1000 masks with `IMS` and unmasks only the receive causes with `IMC` (the old `IMC = u32::MAX` unmasked everything). `core.net_stats` (builtin 73, no opcode) and `base/net_perf` are the counters/proof | spec 0020 N4/N19/N22, ADR 0051, M10.6 |
| The text shell is a thin command loop over separate `base/` apps: it keeps a display cwd (`/` start), prompts `igis:/> `/`igis:/base> `, has a stateful `cd` builtin, and resolves a non-builtin command as `base/<cmd>` (then `system/<cmd>`). The file commands (`ls`, `dir`, `pwd`, `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `mkdir`, `rmdir`, `rm`) receive args `"<cwd> <rest>"`; every other app receives the raw tail, so `nc`, `purl`, `text`, `child` and the rest keep their `run` interfaces. `run` remains the explicit path launcher and keeps its `child:` messages. Harness prompt waits accept both `shell> ` and `igis:/...> ` | spec 0012, spec 0013, M10 (`0b8ff2c`, `3597d6c`, `e02fc8f`) |

## Status log

| Date | Change |
|------|--------|
| 2026-11-03 | Repository created; documentation structure established. |
| 2026-11-03 | Vision captured in spec 0001; ADR 0001 (ring 0 only) and ADR 0002 (interpreted apps) accepted. |
| 2026-11-03 | Architecture spec 0002, language seed 0003; ADR 0003 (pinned cooperative tasks), 0004 (static memory), 0005 (bytecode), 0006 (MMU hardening, provisional). |
| 2026-11-03 | Memory model draft 0004; placement fork (static vs monotonic regions) left open. |
| 2026-11-03 | Option A selected: fully static placement, no runtime allocation (ADR 0007). Memory spec updated with rules, examples, and accepted costs. |
| 2026-11-03 | Destination-form construction recorded: `dst = a + b` writes into a declared buffer; no temporaries, no GC. |
| 2026-11-03 | `exit(reason)` as top-level catch: frames abandon to main level, handler may reclaim or transfer to a validated recovery label (ADR 0008 amended by 0009); aliasing forbidden, no views, capacity-typed strings. |
| 2026-11-03 | Handlers split: `fault(code)` decides terminate/recover, `exit(reason)` always tears down; one recovery per task, no retry (ADR 0010, supersedes 0009). |
| 2026-11-12 | Origin recorded: 2005 front-end for a modem; the ring boundary was the friction that produced ring 0. Execution changed to per-core interpreter loops with work stealing (ADR 0011). Rust, x86_64-first, GRUB/multiboot2 and read-only ext2 recorded (ADR 0012–0015). Specs 0001–0004 updated; host interface (0005), streams (0006) and scheduler (0007) added. |
| 2026-11-12 | Realtime class added: one-way promotion, no quantum preemption, admission by core count, one-second heartbeat with `WatchdogTimeout` (ADR 0016). |
| 2026-11-12 | Realtime admission deliberately allows occupying every core; no core is reserved for the REPL. Tradeoff recorded in the ADR 0016 addendum. |
| 2026-11-12 | REPL model settled: a kernel service compiles and starts applications from a request queue; the shell is an ordinary application; `run`/`spawn`/`wait`/`kill` with global task ids (ADR 0017). |
| 2026-11-12 | REPL details settled: `shell` then `entrypoint`, `no entry` halt, fixed `string[1024]` arguments, queue capacity 1024, orphan children continue (ADR 0017 addendum). |
| 2026-11-12 | Per-task handles replaced by global `TaskId`s and a 64-entry exit-reason ring; no per-task handle table (ADR 0017 addendum 2). |
| 2026-11-12 | Argument delivery settled as `main(args: const string[1024])`; a full request queue returns `Status.Full`, and `run` blocks only on the child. |
| 2026-11-12 | Remaining unresolved items parked in `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`; session paused before code. |
| 2026-11-13 | Host interface settled: console is a byte stream, one global nanosecond clock (no TSC), caller-owned compile diagnostics; Q5–Q7 closed (specs 0003, 0005, 0007). |
| 2026-11-13 | Streams settled: unique application-wide names, one sender and one receiver, `stdlib` framing helpers; explicit close deferred to v2; Q11–Q13 closed (spec 0006). |
| 2026-11-13 | Floating point added to the first milestone: `f64` with a per-task FXSAVE region (ADR 0018). Language settled: no visibility modifiers, `T(x)` casts, FNV-1a maps, `core.bit_*`, `Diagnostic` only; Q15–Q23 closed (specs 0002–0004, 0006, 0007). |
| 2026-11-13 | Boot, loader and REPL settled: sub-1 MiB trampoline, mandatory framebuffer tag, one IPI vector, ext2 subset, dispatcher timing, `ExitReason.Killed`, path validation; Q24–Q38 closed (specs 0002, 0003, 0005, 0007). |
| 2026-11-13 | Spec 0008 drafted: one bytecode package per application, fixed 4-byte-word stack VM, `compiler`/`interpreter` split over `abi`, structural verification at load. |
| 2026-11-13 | Source-only delivery made permanent: no precompiled packages ever, compile latency is not a constraint (ADR 0019; specs 0002, 0008). |
| 2026-11-13 | Specs 0001–0008 accepted. Workspace created (`arch/x86_64`, `kernel`, `xtask`): GRUB/multiboot2 boots into long mode, serial works, the framebuffer tag is parsed and the screen cleared. |
| 2026-11-13 | Kernel diagnostics: 8x8 bitmap-font console on the framebuffer (mirrored to serial), IDT with 32 exception gates and a register dump (verified with a deliberate `int3`), multiboot2 memory-map parsing. |
| 2026-11-13 | Frame allocator: 4 KiB bitmap over the first 4 GiB, initialised from the multiboot2 map and reserving low memory, the kernel image, the multiboot2 structure and the framebuffer; verified by allocating, writing, reading back and freeing two frames (254 MiB usable under `-m 256M`). |
| 2026-11-13 | GDT/TSS with a double-fault IST (verified with a real non-canonical-stack fault), local APIC enabled with the legacy PIC masked, timer calibrated against the PIT (~62.5k ticks/ms in QEMU), and a 1 kHz tick advancing the global nanosecond clock (Q6, spec 0007). |
| 2026-11-13 | Host language slice: `abi` (ABI v1 opcodes/headers), `compiler` (lexer → parser → type/capacity check → deterministic package), `interpreter` (structural verifier, allocation-free machine, `Host` trait), `langhost` runner; 50 tests, kernel untouched. Spec 0008 open questions 1 and 3 resolved. |

| 2026-11-14 | Enums and the `Status` ABI: `type Name = (Variant, ...)` declares a nominal enum whose variants are global ordinal constants (`0..n-1`) in one 8-byte slot; assignment/comparison only within the same enum (ordering by ordinal), `int(enum)` yields the ordinal, `EnumName(int)` is range-checked and faults `Overflow` (opcode `enum_cast`, 60 → 61). `Status` is predeclared with `Ok` = 0; a user `type Status` and a duplicate type/variant are compile errors. `core.spawn(path, args, var id)`, `core.wait(id, var reason)` and `core.kill(id)` now return `Status`; the reason stays a staged `int` and `Diagnostic`/`ExitReason` records remain missing (Q43 narrowed). The shell checks each `Status` and prints `spawn failed`/`wait failed` plus ok/killed/faulted. |
| 2026-11-14 | Records and the record-based REPL ABI: `type Name = record field: T; ... end;` with nominal types, declaration-order 8-byte-aligned layout, nested records, buffer fields, whole-record copies (`copy_refs`), field references (`ref_offset`) and `value`/`const`/`var` record parameters. `Diagnostic` and `ExitReason` are predeclared at the fixed `abi::layout` offsets; `core.spawn(path, args, var diag, var id)` fills `diag` from the kernel compiler on a synchronous failure and clears it otherwise, and `core.wait(id, var reason)` writes `kind`/`code`. `run missing` prints `spawn failed: No such application` from `diag.message`, `run faulty` reports `child: faulted` from `reason.kind`, and the staged reason `int` is gone (Q43 narrowed to sum types and `core.run`). |
| 2026-11-14 | Sum types, `case` and the real `ExitReason`: `type Name = Variant | Variant(payload: T);` is nominal, an 8-byte tag at offset 0 plus the largest payload at offset 8, constructed into a destination (`dst := Variant(payload)`; variant names are scoped to the sum, so `Ok` is `Status.Ok` or `ExitReason.Ok` by destination). `case value of Variant: ... Variant(binding): ... end;` is exhaustive (one arm per variant, no `else`), has no fallthrough, and copies a payload by value into an arm-local binding; missing, duplicate and unknown arms, payload mismatches and cross-sum variants are compile errors. No new opcodes: construction is `store_ref`/`clear`/`append`/`copy_refs`, `case` is `load_ref`/`eq`/`jz`. `ExitReason` becomes `Ok | Killed | Faulted(code: FaultCode)` at the same 16-byte layout; `FaultCode` is a predeclared enum with qualified variant names (`FaultCode.DivideByZero`) because two names collide with `Status`. The shell's `report` is an exhaustive `case`; `run faulty` prints `child: faulted DivideByZero`, `run child world` prints `child: ok`, `run missing` keeps its `diag.message`, and the kernel run ends `task 1: exit Ok`. Q43 narrows to `core.run`, `TaskId` and the request-completion diagnostic path. |

| 2026-11-14 | Fixed-size arrays: `array[T, N]` (positive const N) in state, locals, records, sum payloads and `value`/`const`/`var` parameters. Layout is N elements at `align8(element size)` with the array rounded to 8; arrays are nominal in element type and count. `a[i]` reads/writes the element (`index < N` at run time, `fault(OutOfBounds)`, never a clamp), nested `a[i][j]`/`a[i].field` composes, whole-array assignment and `value` parameters copy `N * stride` bytes, and `const`/`var` parameters are call-scoped references. One new opcode `ref_element` (immediates stride, count; ABI v1 61 → 62) forms an element reference; the verifier requires an 8-byte-multiple stride and a non-zero count whose product fits a section. One structural array table is shared by the app and `stdlib`, so arrays cross the unit boundary; `stdlib.split_all` fills an `array[string[64], 8]`. `samples/lang/array.ig` and `array_bounds.ig` cover reads, writes, copies, records, nested arrays, buffers, parameters, the stdlib helper and the bounds fault. |
| 2026-11-14 | Fixed-capacity rings: `ring[T, N]` (positive const N) in state, locals, records, sum payloads, arrays and `value`/`const`/`var` parameters; nominal in element and count; layout is the `N` elements at `align8(element size)` followed by `head: u64` and `count: u64`; whole-ring copies include the header. `core.ring_push`/`pop`/`peek`/`full`/`empty` are compiler-lowered over `ref_offset`/`load_ref`/`store_ref`/`copy_refs`/`ref_element`, `add_u`/`mod_u` and `eq`+`jz`, so the ABI v1 opcode table is unchanged. `push` on full returns `Full` and writes nothing; `pop`/`peek` on empty return `NotFound` and leave the out-parameter untouched; `push`/`pop` require a writable place, so `const` is rejected while a `value` copy is writable and private to the callee. The structural ring table is shared with `stdlib` (`drain_into` takes a `ring[int, 8]`); `samples/lang/ring.ig` covers FIFO order, peek, full/empty, wrap-around, whole copies and buffer/record/array/nested-ring elements. |
| 2026-11-15 | Fixed-capacity maps: `map[K, V, N]` (`N` a positive const, `K` one of `int`, `string[M]`, `bytes[M]`) in state, locals, records, sum payloads, array and ring elements, and `value`/`const`/`var` parameters; nominal in `(K, V, N)`, no at-least-N, no comparison. Layout is `count: u64` then `capacity` (smallest power of two at least `2N`) slots of `state`/`hash`/key/value; the hash is FNV-1a over the eight little-endian bytes of an `int` key or the `[0..len)` bytes of a buffer key, probing is linear and deletion is backward-shift with no tombstones. `core.map_put`/`get`/`has`/`remove`/`count` are five new opcodes (`MAP_PUT` … `MAP_COUNT`, key and value kinds in the opcode word, `capacity`/`count`/`key_size`/`value_size` immediates), so the algorithm lives once in the interpreter; `put` at `N` entries returns `Full` and writes nothing, a missing key is `NotFound`/`false`, and `get` leaves its out-parameter untouched. Each `ApStack` is now guard + 256 KiB body; all seven AP guards are installed and PTE-checked before the SIPIs, and `smp::start` prints `guards: boot 1, ap 7, tables M`, halting on a mismatch or a changed free-frame count. | 2026-11-16 | Fixed-point `f64` text (spec 0003, "Floating-point text"; spec 0008). `core.format_f64_into(dst, value, frac_digits) -> int` is one new `FORMAT_F64` opcode (`a` = destination buffer kind, a non-zero capacity immediate; destination reference, `f64` bits and `frac_digits` on the stack) implemented as a pure buffer operation in the interpreter: it builds the digits in a local array and publishes them with one copy, so a rejected value never leaves a partial destination. At most 9 fraction digits (`-4` beyond), half-away-from-zero rounding with the carry applied to the integer part (`9.999` with 2 → `10.00`), `nan`/`inf`/`-inf` written literally, `-4` for `\|value\| >= 2^63` because the form has no exponent, and `-6` when the result does not fit (destination length untouched). The sign of `-0.0` survives (`-0.000`). `stdlib.parse_f64_into(src, var value) -> Status` is ordinary language code: `[+-]? ( digits [ '.' digits? ] \| '.' digits ) ( [eE] [+-]? digits )?` plus the formatter's `inf`/`+inf`/`-inf`/`nan`; everything else is `Invalid` and writes nothing. The fraction accumulates as an integer numerator over a power of ten (18 significant digits, so `0.375` is exact) and the exponent is applied by repeated ×/÷10 with an early exit at saturation, so `1e400` is `±Inf` and `1e-400` is `±0`, both with `Ok`. Tests: interpreter hex/float formatter cases (specials, carry, `-0.0`, `2^63`, capacity and kind errors), compiler arity/type/encoding cases, `langhost` valid/malformed/round-trip cases, the stdlib helper count (21), `samples/lang/f64text.ig` on the host and `samples/apps/floats` in the kernel (`run floats 3.25` → `value=3.250`, `run floats nope` → `Invalid`). ABI v1 grows from 71 to 72 opcodes; Q41 narrows to shortest round-trip formatting and exact IEEE parsing. |
| 2026-11-16 | Shortest round-trip `f64` text (spec 0003, "Floating-point text"; spec 0008; Q41). `core.format_f64_short_into(dst, value) -> int` is one new `FORMAT_F64_SHORT` opcode (`a` = destination buffer kind, a non-zero capacity immediate, no width operand) implemented as an exact search in `interpreter/src/f64short.rs`: the value and both ends of its round-trip interval are expanded to base-`10^9` integers (the value times `5^k` and `2^shift`; at most 769 digits, 88 limbs, `5^27` and `2^30` chunk multiplication), then for `n = 1..=17` the two `n`-digit decimals that bracket the value are compared against the interval endpoints, with endpoint inclusivity from the significand's low bit (round-half-to-even) and the lower half-gap halved at a power-of-two boundary. The first `n` that lands inside wins; when both candidates do, the nearer one is taken and an exact tie rounds away from zero, matching the host reference. Shape: positional while `-6 < e10 + 1 <= 21` (`1e20`, `1e-6`, `0.000001`), scientific otherwise (`1e21`, `1e-7`, `5e-324`, `1.7976931348623157e308`), no trailing `.0`, `0`/`-0`, `nan`, `inf`/`-inf` literal, `-6` with the length untouched when the text does not fit. The text is staged in a local array and published with one copy: no allocation, no host call, no floating-point arithmetic after the decode, and no data tables beyond a 72-byte `10^0..=10^8` array (~9.4 KiB of `.text` at `-O`, stack frame 5.7 KiB). Tests: `interpreter/tests/f64short.rs` compares the produced string against the shape rule applied to Rust's `{:e}` for 24 000+ xorshift-bit values (all finite ones) plus every power of ten and two with both neighbours, every biased exponent at eight mantissa boundaries in both signs, and a hard-value list, and independently re-parses every result; `verify.rs` covers the hand-assembled cases and the kind/capacity/reserved-field rejections; `compiler/tests/compile.rs` covers arity, destination kinds and encoding; `samples/lang/f64short.ig` runs on the host and `samples/apps/floats` prints `s:third`, `s:1e21`, `s:tinysub` and `s:max` in the kernel. The ABI v1 table holds **75** opcodes (counted in `abi/src/opcodes.rs`; the `71 to 72` figure in the row above was off by two, and spec 0008's `73 to 74` for `format_f64` was already correct). Q41 narrows to correctly rounded decimal-to-binary parsing and its subnormal edges. |

Without the split, a console loop (the new `nc`) selected an empty established socket, then blocked in `recv` where a console byte could no longer wake it; `task::source_ready` and `KernelHost::socket_readable` now use `select_ready`. | 2026-11-20 | Terminal scrolling no longer trips the normal-task watchdog (spec 0021 T2; user report "terminal freezes and crashes when it has to scroll down (cursor reaches end of the text area)"). `apps/utilities/terminal` kept the screen as `array[string[MaxLine], MaxRows]` and `scroll_one` copied every row (`lines[i] := lines[i + 1]`), so one scroll copied 47 × 256 bytes and a large output burst (e.g. `cat apps/productivity/browser`, ~143 KiB, ~1,800 scrolls) burned the task's 10 s / 200 M-step budget and the task exited `Faulted(BudgetExceeded)`. The grid is now a ring: `top` names logical row 0, `phys(row) = (top + row) mod rows` indexes it, a scroll advances `top` and clears the one row that becomes the new bottom. Insert/backspace/draw use `phys`. The terminal survives the largest source and still answers `echo terminal-alive`; `scripts/terminal_scroll_qmp.py` proves it on `-smp 4`/`-smp 1` (no terminal `Faulted(BudgetExceeded)` line, prompt returns, terminal responsive), and `multi_terminal_qmp.py` still passes. 610 host tests; no ABI/`stdlib` change. |

The clock's live mode now blocks in the new `core.sleep(ms)` builtin 79 instead of a realtime spin: `Host::blocking_sleep` defaults false (mocks/`langhost` immediate `Ok`), the kernel arms `WaitKind::Deadline` and the machine keeps the absolute deadline across the retry; blocked time is not budgeted and a busy realtime clock can no longer starve input. `clock_window` prints `ready HH:MM:SS` as a diagnostic. Verified on `-smp 4`/`-smp 1`: `run clock_window check`, `clock_window shot` (host time, no fault), live launch (face visible, closes on the close box), `run clock` RTC proof, 613 host tests, and the regenerated README captures with host times. |

| 2026-11-22 | M15.2 attaches the application volume (spec 0025, ADR 0059; user request "GRUB yalnızca ve yalnızca kerneli yüklemeli"). The 128 MiB ext2 volume is now a real disk at `ide0` unit 1; `ata.rs` gains `Unit { Master, Slave }`, a per-command drive value and one `CHANNEL` lock (both units share `0x1F0..=0x1F7`), so `init(Unit)` identifies and logs both drives. `xtask run` makes home `index=0` and attaches `apps.img`/`apps-shell.img` at `index=1` with `snapshot=on`: QEMU's `ide-hd` refuses `readonly=on` ("Block node is read-only"), and spec 0025 B12 was amended to the overlay, which leaves the file byte-identical (md5 checked). One `apps_drive(iso)` helper in `scripts/tablet_qmp.py` maps the ISO to its matching image; all 40 QEMU harnesses use it. The kernel still boots `system/entrypoint` from the GRUB module and a boot probe mounts the slave read-only and prints `apps: volume ok (7 root entries, base/shell 15416 bytes)`. Proof: 634 host tests (0 failed; `RUST_MIN_STACK=16777216` needed only because `langhost`'s editor test overflows the default test-thread stack here), `-smp 4`/`-smp 1`/`--no-home`/`--shell` transcripts showing `ata: primary slave, LBA28, 262144 sectors`, `boot_desktop_qmp.py` (its stale Apps row 10 → 11 fixed; row 10 is the `#Utilities` header), `home_persistence_qmp.py` and `image_viewer_qmp.py`; `e2fsck -fn` clean on `apps.img`, `apps-shell.img`, `home.img`. | 2026-11-22 | M15.4 offset-independent reads (spec 0025 B19/B20, ADR 0059 amendment). `ext2w` gains a per-inode logical-to-physical resolver, `block_at(inode, index)`, that reads only the pointer path a logical block needs: direct `blocks[0..12]`, single-indirect `blocks[12]`, then the double-indirect outer and inner block. `read_at` now computes the first and last logical block the requested range touches and resolves only those, keeping the old semantics for `offset >= size` (`Ok(0)`), a short read at EOF, an empty destination and a zero pointer (`Invalid`); an index at or past `ceil(size/1024)` and a triple-indirect file stay `IoError`. `read_file` and the directory walkers keep `each_data_block`. New host test `reads_past_the_direct_blocks_by_offset` stages a 2 MiB file in an 8 MiB image and compares direct, single- and double-indirect reads, a block-crossing range, EOF/past-EOF and a 3-byte large-offset read against `read_file` and a chunked sequential loop. Measured on `-smp 4`: `image_viewer_qmp.py` **48.2 s** end to end (329 s before; B20 gate 120 s), `image_viewer: ready 4608x2963 -> 576x371`; boot-to-ready 1.78 s (1.70 s before, unchanged within measurement). Proof: **635 host tests** (0 failed; 556 language + 56 `net` + 23 `ext2w`), `home_persistence_qmp.py` (`home_verify: ok`, fsck 12/2048 files, 563/8192 blocks after the reset), `boot_desktop_qmp.py` (`terminal: ok`), `e2fsck -fn` clean on `apps.img` (81/8192 files, 125062/131072 blocks) and `home.img`. `CACHE_BLOCKS` stays 8: the seek fix removed the visible metadata cost, so no cache growth was needed. No ABI change; ABI v1 stays 75. |
